F. 2003 — 584 [2003/200089] 9 JANUARY 2003. — Decree of the Walloon Government approving the rules of procedure of the Regional Council of mental health services

The Walloon Government, Having regard to the decree of 4 April 1996 organizing the approval and subsidization of mental health services, in particular Article 24; Having regard to the decree of the Walloon Government of 7 November 1996 implementing the decree of 4 April 1996 organizing the approval and subsidization of mental health services, in particular section 12; Having regard to the decree of the Walloon Government of 27 August 2001 regulating the operation of the Government; Having regard to the decree of the Walloon Government of 27 August 2001 determining the distribution of powers between Ministers and regulating the signature of acts of the Government; Having regard to the decree of the Walloon Government of 24 October 2002 appointing the members of the Regional Council of mental health services; Considering the rules of procedure established by the Regional Council of mental health services at its meeting of 23 December 2002;

Considering that it is the responsibility of the Walloon Government to approve the rules of procedure of the Regional Council of mental health services;

On the proposal of the Minister of Social Affairs and Health;

After deliberation, Decrees:

#### Article 1er. This decree regulates, in application of Article 138 of the Constitution, a matter referred to in

Article 128, § 1er, thereof.

#### Art. 2. The Walloon Government approves the rules of procedure of the Regional Council of mental health

services, appearing in the annex to this decree.

#### Art. 3. The decree of 20 February 1997 approving the rules of procedure of the Regional Council of mental

health services is repealed.

#### Art. 4. This decree enters into force on the date of its adoption by the Government.

#### Art. 5.

The Minister of Social Affairs and Health is responsible for the implementation of this decree.

Namur, 9 January 2003.

The Minister-President, J.-Cl. VANCAUWENBERGHE

The Minister of Social Affairs and Health, Th. DETIENNE

Annex

REGIONAL COUNCIL OF MENTAL HEALTH SERVICES

Rules of procedure

CHAPTER Ier.—General provisions

#### Article 1er. The opinion requested from the Council by the Minister shall be given, unless otherwise provided, within two months unless

the Government has set another time limit.

Upon reasoned request, the Minister may request an urgent opinion. In this case, he shall set a reasonable time limit.

#### Art. 2.

At the beginning of each calendar year, the Council shall fix the calendar of ordinary meetings.

## CHAPTER II.—Convocations

#### Art. 3. The President shall convene the members of the Council as well as persons who attend the meetings with

consultative vote.

#### Art. 4. The convocation is sent to the members at least eight days before the meeting and systematically contains

a proxy form. If it has not been sent beforehand, the minutes of the previous meeting shall be attached to the convocation. It shall state the agenda of the meeting. The convocations are sent to the address notified by the members. They shall indicate the place and time of the meeting. The period of eight days may be reduced in case of urgency.

#### Art. 5. The agenda is fixed by the bureau.

At the opening of the meeting, the agenda as well as the minutes of the previous meeting are approved.

The agenda may be modified or supplemented.

## CHAPTER III.—The Council

#### Art. 6. § 1er. The Council may validly issue an opinion or make a report only on condition that at least half

of the members are present or represented.

Absent members may be represented by proxy. Each member present may hold only one proxy. Decisions are taken by simple majority. In case of a tie, the President's vote is decisive.

If the quorum of attendance is not met, the members are convened again, at the latest within the following fortnight. For the item re-entered on the agenda, a simple majority of the members present is required. If a member is absent from the Council three times in succession, he shall be questioned by the President who may, depending on the explanations given by the member concerned, propose his resignation to the Minister.

MONITEUR BELGE — 12.02.2003 — BELGISCH STAATSBLAD 7061

#### § 2. The result of the vote is attached to the opinion; at its request, the minority may attach to the majority opinion a note

specifying its point of view.

#### Art. 7.

Only items on the agenda attached to the convocation may be validly submitted to a vote. However, in case of extreme urgency duly justified, an item may be added to the agenda during the meeting as soon as the members present have agreed to examine it.

#### Art. 8. No member of the Council, directly interested in a request for an opinion submitted to the Council - because he is a

member of the organizing authority or staff of the requesting institution - may take part in the debate and vote on the opinion concerning this request. In the event that the reality of a direct interest on the part of one or more member(s) is disputed, the Council shall deliberate in the presence of this (these) member(s).

In case of a tie, the decision belongs to the President.

#### Art. 9. If the Council deems it necessary, it may invite the Mental Health Service whose file is on the agenda

in order to obtain additional or supplementary information.

#### Art. 10. The Council may call upon experts whom it designates.

## CHAPTER IV.—The bureau

#### Art. 11.

§ 1er. Within the Regional Council of mental health services, hereinafter referred to as the Council, a bureau is established responsible for the organization and coordination of the work.

The bureau prepares the meetings of the Council and ensures the transmission of proposals or opinions adopted by the Council. The bureau may, in addition to the Minister's requests for opinions, itself formulate requests for opinions and submit them to the Council.

#### § 2. The bureau is composed of the President and the two vice-presidents designated by the Walloon Government by

decree of 24 October 2002.

The vice-presidents ensure, in turn, the chairmanship of the meetings in case of inability of the president.

#### Art. 12. The two representatives of the Government are invited to the meetings of the bureau.

#### Art. 13. The bureau may call all or part of the members of the Council for its work.

## CHAPTER V.—Working groups

#### Art. 14. To fulfill its mission, the Council may establish working groups, either permanent or temporary

on the occasion of a specific problem or within the framework of a request for an opinion formulated by the Government or the

Minister.

Within the framework of these working groups, it may call upon experts.

#### Art. 15. The members of the Council may be part of the working groups.

The members - external to the Council - invited to a specific working group are so by virtue of their competence and in a personal capacity, on the proposal of the president of the working group and with the agreement of the bureau. The bureau, after having taken the opinion of the president of the working group, may be led to limit the number of members of the working groups according to the matters studied. The designation of experts is the subject of a decision of the Council.

#### Art. 16. With the exception of minutes, the documents of the working groups are sent only to the

members of these groups.

The other members of the Council may take cognizance of the documents at the Secretariat upon request.

The member who is absent from the meetings of a working group three times consecutively, is considered as having resigned.

#### Art. 17. The Secretariat of the Council practically organizes the activities of the working groups, to the extent of the

means allocated to it by the Government.

#### Art. 18. The presidents of the permanent working groups are designated by the Council on the proposal of the bureau

for a period of three years renewable once.

They regularly report to the Council on the progress of the work, for approval.

The President of the Council may attend as of right all the meetings of the different working groups and possibly chair them.

#### Art. 19.

A quarterly report of each working group is transmitted to the bureau and to the Council.

Seen to be annexed to the decree of the Walloon Government of 9 January 2003 approving the internal regulations of the Regional Council of mental health services.

Namur, 9 January 2003.

The Minister-President, J.-Cl. VANCAUWENBERGHE

The Minister of Social Affairs and Health, Th. DETIENNE

MINISTRY OF THE WALLOON REGION

N. 2003 — 584 [2003/200089] 9 JANUARY 2003.—Decree of the Walloon Government approving the internal regulations of the Regional Council of mental health services

The Walloon Government, Having regard to the decree of the Walloon Region of 4 April 1996 concerning the recognition and subsidizing of mental health services, in particular Article 24;

7062 BELGIAN OFFICIAL GAZETTE — 12.02.2003 — BELGIAN OFFICIAL GAZETTE

Having regard to the decree of the Walloon Government of 7 November 1996 implementing the decree of 4 April 1996 concerning the recognition and subsidizing of mental health services, in particular section 12;

Having regard to the decree of the Walloon Government of 27 August 2001 regulating its operation;

Having regard to the decree of the Walloon Government of 27 August 2001 establishing the distribution of ministerial powers and regulating the signature of its acts; Having regard to the decree of the Walloon Government of 24 October 2002 appointing the members of the Regional Council of mental health services;

Having regard to the internal regulations drawn up by the Regional Council of mental health services during its meeting of 23 December 2002;

Considering that the internal regulations of the Regional Council of mental health services must be approved by the Walloon Government; On the proposal of the Minister of Social Affairs and Health, After deliberation, Decrees:

#### Article 1.

This decree regulates, in accordance with Article 138 of the Constitution, a matter referred to in Article 128, § 1, of the Constitution.

#### Art. 2.

The internal regulations of the Regional Council of mental health services, which accompany this decree, are approved by the Walloon Government.

#### Art. 3. The decree of 20 February 1997 approving the internal regulations of the Regional Council

of mental health services is repealed.

#### Art. 4. This decree enters into force on the day it is adopted by the Government.

#### Art. 5.

The Minister of Social Affairs and Health is responsible for the implementation of this decree.

Annex

REGIONAL COUNCIL OF MENTAL HEALTH SERVICES

Internal regulations

## CHAPTER I.—General provisions

#### Article 1. Unless otherwise provided, the opinion requested from the Council by the Minister is given within

two months provided that the Government has not established another time limit.

Upon reasoned request, the Minister may request urgent advice. In that case, he establishes a reasonable time limit.

#### Art. 2.

The Council determines the calendar of ordinary meetings at the beginning of each calendar year.

## CHAPTER II.—Convocations

#### Art. 3. The president convenes the members of the Council, as well as the persons who attend the meetings with an advisory

vote.

#### Art. 4. The convocation is sent to the members at least eight days before the meeting and systematically contains

a proxy form. The minutes of the previous meeting are attached to the convocation if they have not been sent earlier. The convocation indicates the agenda of the meeting. The convocations are sent to the address that the members have communicated. They indicate the place and time of the meeting. In urgent cases, the eight-day period may be shortened.

#### Art. 5. The agenda is determined by the bureau.

The agenda and the minutes of the previous meeting are approved at the opening of the meeting. The agenda may be amended or supplemented.

## CHAPTER III.—The Council

#### Art. 6. § 1. The opinion or report of the Council is only validly issued if at least half

of the members are present or represented.

Absent members may be represented by proxy. Each member present may only hold one proxy. Decisions are taken by simple majority. In case of a tie, the vote of the president is decisive.

If the attendance quorum is not reached, the members are convened again at the latest within the next fourteen days. A point that is again included on the agenda requires a simple majority of the members present. A member who does not participate in the Council three times consecutively is challenged by the president, who may propose his resignation to the Minister based on the explanation given.

#### § 2. The result of the vote is attached to the opinion; the minority may, upon request, attach to the opinion of

the majority a note in which it indicates its position.

#### Art. 7. Only the agenda items attached to the convocation may validly be submitted to the vote.

BELGIAN OFFICIAL GAZETTE — 12.02.2003 — BELGIAN OFFICIAL GAZETTE 7063

In case of duly justified urgent necessity, a point may nevertheless be added to the agenda during the session if the members present are willing to examine it.

#### Art. 8. A member of the Council who has a direct interest in a request for an opinion submitted to the Council-

namely as a member of the founding authority or of the staff of the requesting institution-may not participate in the debate nor in the vote concerning the opinion in relation to that request.

If it is disputed that one or more members have a direct interest, the Council deliberates on it in the presence of the member(s) concerned.

In case of a tie, the president decides.

#### Art. 9. If the Council deems it necessary, it may invite the mental health service whose file is on the

agenda in order to obtain additional or supplementary information.

#### Art.10.TheCouncilmaycalluponexpertswhomitappoints.

## CHAPTERIV.—Thebureau

#### Art. 11.

§ 1. Within the Regional council for mental health services, hereinafter referred to as "the Council", a bureau is established which is responsible for the organisation and coordination of the work.

The bureau prepares the meetings of the Council and ensures the communication of the proposals or opinions that it approves.

In addition to requests for advice from the Minister, the bureau may itself formulate requests for advice and submit them to the Council.

#### §2.Thebureauiscomposedofthechairmanandtwovice-chairmenappointedbydecreeoftheWalloon

Governmentof24October2002.

In the absence of the chairman, the chairmanship of the meetings is assumed alternately by the vice-chairmen.

#### Art.12.

The two representatives of the Government are invited to the meetings of the bureau.

#### Art.13.ThebureaumaycalluponsomeorallofthemembersoftheCounciltoparticipateinitswork.

## CHAPTERV.—WorkingGroups

#### Art.14.Inordertofulfilitstask,theCouncilmayestablisheitherpermanentortemporaryworkinggroupsfora

specificproblemorinthecontextofarequeestforadviceformulatedbytheGovernmentorbytheMinister.

In the context of those working groups, it may call upon experts.

#### Art.15.ThemembersoftheCouncilmaybepartoftheworkinggroups.

The members of a working group who do not belong to the Council are personally invited on the proposal of the chairman of the working group and with the consent of the bureau on the basis of their competence.

Depending on the matters dealt with, the bureau may, after advice from the chairman, be led to limit the number of members of the working groups.

The experts are appointed by decision of the Council.

#### Art. 16. The documents of the working groups are only sent to the members of those working groups, with

the exception of the minutes. The other members of the Council who request them may consult the documents at the secretariat. The member who misses three consecutive meetings of a working group is deemed to have resigned.

#### Art.17.ThesecretariatoftheCouncilcoordinatestheactivitiesoftheworkinggroupsaccordingtothemeans

that the Government allocates to it.

#### Art.18.ThechairmenofthepermanentworkinggroupsareappointedbytheCouncilontheproposalofthebureau

for a term of three years. That term may be renewed once.

They regularly report to the Council on the progress of the work. The Council must approve the report. The chairman of the Council may by right attend all meetings of the various working groups and possibly chair them.

#### Art.19.EachworkinggroupdrawsupaquarterlyreportforthebureauandfortheCouncil.

23 DECEMBER 2002.—Ministerial decree approving the care insurance manual

The Flemish Minister for Welfare, Public Health, Equal Opportunities and Development Cooperation,

Having regard to the decree of 30 March 1999 on the organisation of care insurance, amended by the decrees of 22 December 1999, 8 December 2000, 18 May 2001 and 20 December 2002;

Having regard to the decision of the Flemish Government of 17 July 2000 concerning the organisation, management, operation and recognition of care funds and concerning the supervision of care funds, as amended by the decision of the Flemish Government of 14 December 2001;

Having regard to the decision of the Flemish Government of 28 September 2001 on recognition, registration and authorisation, and on affiliation, application and coverage within the framework of care insurance, as amended by the decisions of the Flemish Government of 14 December 2001, 25 October 2002 and 13 December 2002;

Having regard to the decision of the Flemish Government of 3 July 2002 determining the powers of the members of the Flemish Government;

Having regard to the opinion of the Inspection of Finance, given on 6 December 2002;

Having regard to the laws on the Council of State, coordinated on 12 January 1973, in particular Article 3, § 1, replaced by the law of 4 July 1989 and amended by the law of 4 August 1996;

Having regard to the urgent necessity;

Considering that the Flemish care insurance starts according to the adapted procedures on 1 January 2003 so that immediate effect must be given to the provisions of the decisions of the Flemish Government in this matter and the care funds concerned and the citizens must be provided with legal certainty regarding the new scheme within the framework of care insurance,

Decides:

#### Artikel 1.

The care insurance manual, which is attached as an annex to this decision, is approved.

#### Art. 2. The ministerial decree of 1 October 2001 concerning the approval of the care insurance manual

is repealed.

#### Art. 3. This decree enters into force on 1 January 2003.

Brussels, 23 December 2002.

The Flemish Minister of Welfare, Health, Equal Opportunities and Development Cooperation,

PART I.—Further definition of the actors

## CHAPTER I.—Representative

The representative of the user is a natural person who acts in place of the user in all actions that the user must take in order to obtain coverage of costs for non-medical care within the framework of care insurance.

### Section I.—Representative without power of attorney

The following persons can act as a representative of a user without power of attorney: + the husband, wife or a relative in the first or second degree ((grand)parent, (grand)child, brother or sister) of the user; the representative must state on the application form in what capacity he or she stands in relation to the user (wife, sister, etc.);

+ An adult member of the user's household. In this case, a certificate of household composition of the persons concerned is sufficient as proof. This certificate is issued by the municipality;

MONITEUR BELGE — 12.02.2003 — BELGISCH STAATSBLAD 7069

+ the legal representative of the user: being 1 of the following persons: (cid:226) one of the parents or, where applicable, the guardian for a non-emancipated minor. The same applies to the extended minor who is equated on the level of his person and his property with a minor under fifteen years; (cid:226) the guardian, being where applicable the husband or wife for a person declared incapable; (cid:226) a provisional administrator may be provided for a suspect who is interned and is neither declared incapable nor under guardianship, if his interests so require. The provisional administrator collects among other things claims, settles the debts and represents the internee in law as defendant and as plaintiff; (cid:226) a provisional administrator may be appointed by the justice of the peace to an adult who is wholly or partially, albeit temporarily due to his state of health, unable to manage his property. Unless the contrary appears from the judicial decision appointing the provisional administrator, he represents the protected person in all legal acts and proceedings concerning his property.

### Section II.—Representative with power of attorney

Persons who do not meet one of the descriptions mentioned in section I can act as a representative of the user if they have a document stating that the user or his legal representative grants him power of attorney. This can be anyone, it is not limited to the legal concept of 'representative'.

This document must be dated and signed by the user or his legal representative. The statement on the document may be as follows: 'name user' hereby grants power of attorney to 'name representative' to carry out in the name and on behalf of 'name user' all formalities necessary for him/her to be able to assert his/her right to coverage within the framework of care insurance.

PART II.—Description of the processes

## CHAPTER I.—Amount of the contributions

Period: 1 October 2001 to 31 December 2002: 10 euro year 2003: 25 euro

10 euro for persons who on 1 January 2002 are entitled to an increased allowance from the health care insurance

## CHAPTERII.—Paymentoftheannualcontributionduebyembers

Under this chapter, payment is dealt with once one has become a member. The care fund is obliged to collect the annual membership contributions from its members. The care fund determines the manner in which it collects the contributions from its members. The care fund or the founding institution may send the invitation to pay the contribution for the care insurance together with the invitation to pay for other products (of the care fund or of its founding institutions) via one single document. However, the member shall be provided with a separate notification clearly stating what amount is requested from the member for the Flemish care insurance. It is also clearly stated for which period the amount is requested.

Both documents can of course be sent in the same mailing. The notification must contain at least the following information:

+ the amount requested in the context of the care insurance and the period for which this amount is requested. + the measure of suspension of coverage for four months at the moment when the member is entitled to coverage, for each year that a member has not paid or has not fully paid their contribution. + if a member considers themselves unable to pay the annual membership contribution, they may ask the OCMW whether it is willing to pay the membership contribution to the care fund in the member's place. However, the OCMW is not obliged to comply with this member's request.

Existing direct debit mandates may only be used to have the contribution paid by a member if this person (or their head of household) has given prior consent for this. The member pays their due annual membership contribution in the current year to their care fund. When a person does not pay, the care fund may send a reminder letter to this person. The care funds may set a deadline for payment of the contribution. This deadline may not fall before 1 January of the year to which the contribution relates. If this deadline falls in the current year, no sanctions may be attached to it.

A member for whom an application for coverage in the context of the care insurance is submitted, pays their membership contribution for that year before submitting the application. In derogation from this, the person for whom an application is submitted in the first quarter of the year, pays their contribution before 1 April of that year.

A member who already has the right to coverage, pays their membership contribution in the first quarter of the year. In case of transfer, a member who has changed care fund pays their first contribution to their new care fund in the first quarter of the year. Third parties (employer, OCMW) may pay the contributions of persons who are members of a care fund. The care fund is in possession of a list of persons for whom the third party pays the contribution. The list that the third party provides to the care fund contains, per member, the data that must be kept in the register. This procedure is not mandatory for contributions jointly paid by a head of household.

7070 MONITEUR BELGE — 12.02.2003 — BELGISCH STAATSBLAD

## CHAPTERIII.—Membershipwithacarefund

### SectionI.—Membershipwithacarefundofchoice

A person is only a member of a care fund when the membership contribution has been paid for the first time. The founding institutions of the care funds may send a mailing to their members and/or insured persons with the notification that they may join a care fund. This mailing may be done in the name of the founding institution. On the one hand there is the notification concerning the care insurance and on the other hand the invitation to pay the contribution through which one becomes a member. The mailing may be done together with the promotion of other products.

The notification and the invitation to pay (the transfer) must be distributed on separate documents.

The notification must contain at least the following information:

+ the amount requested in the context of the care insurance and the period for which this amount is requested; + everyone who resides in Flanders and is older than 25 years is obliged to join one of the eight recognised care funds. This is done by paying the membership contribution; + the measure of suspension of coverage for four months at the moment when the member is entitled to coverage, for each year that a member has not paid or has not fully paid their contribution; + if a member considers themselves unable to pay the annual membership contribution, they may ask the OCMW whether it is willing to pay the membership contribution to the care fund in the member's place. However, the OCMW is not obliged to comply with this member's request. The invitation to pay only states the amount to be paid for the care insurance. It is emphasised that the first contribution, through which one becomes a member, must be paid by deposit to an account number in the name of the care fund.

The care funds must respect the user's freedom of choice. The notification and the invitation to pay may therefore not contain any indications that suggest that membership of the care fund in question is mandatory. It must always be clear that membership of one of the eight recognised care funds is mandatory.

The care funds may set 1 April at the earliest as the deadline for payment of the contribution, stating the reason that otherwise one will be affiliated ex officio with the Flemish care fund from 1 July. In no case may the suspension of four months be given as a sanction for this.

With the consent of the persons concerned, third parties may enrol persons with a care fund. The care funds that enrol persons through third parties have a declaration from the person concerned showing that they voluntarily choose to join the care fund. If a third party wishes to realise the enrolment of other persons, the third party draws up a list of the persons who wish to be enrolled with the care fund through the third party concerned. The care fund will then have in its possession a list of enrolled persons for whom payment is made as a group. This procedure is not mandatory for joint enrolments paid by the head of household.

Only an employer or an OCMW may act as a third party.

A head of household may enrol household members.

The list that the third party provides to the care fund contains, per member, the data that must be kept in the register (national register number, name, address, date of birth, gender, nationality). The third parties provide a list to the care fund of the persons for whom payment is made, together with payment of the contributions.

The care fund must keep a register of the persons enrolled with it. It registers at least the following data of the members: u national register number u name u address u date of birth u gender u nationality u date of payment of first membership contribution The care fund itself determines what form this register will take. The Care Fund only requires that these data be kept on site per individual member in a comprehensible form so that they can be checked by an inspector.

When a person does not join, a reminder letter may be sent.

### Section II.—Ex officio enrollment

A first payment of the contribution is not a condition for ex officio enrollment with the Flemish care fund.

The Flemish care fund must enroll ex officio all persons who do not enroll within the specified deadlines with a recognized care fund:

In case of ex officio enrollment in 2003, the person receives an invitation to pay the contribution for the years 2002 and 2003. If the person enrolls with the Flemish care fund in the same year (the year 2003) and pays the contribution for the years 2002 and 2003, then no suspension is imposed on the person.

The Flemish care fund will enroll ex officio the person who has not enrolled with a care fund within the specified deadline and will inform him of this immediately and in writing. This letter will, in addition to an invitation to pay the membership contribution to the Flemish care fund, also contain the notification that the person concerned can still enroll with a recognized care fund of his choice. If the person still enrolls with another care fund, the Flemish Care Fund will inform the Flemish care fund of this. In that case, the Flemish care fund cancels the ex officio enrollment. If the person concerned enrolls in the years following the year in which he was first obliged to, then the care fund with which the person still enrolls (with the exception of the Flemish care fund in the year 2003 if payment is made for 2002 and 2003) must impose a suspension on him due to non-payment of the annual contributions. The care fund registers the suspension of this person.

MONITEUR BELGE — 12.02.2003 — BELGISCH STAATSBLAD 7071

The Flemish Care Fund will inform the care fund if it should appear that the person was already enrolled with another care fund.

If the enrollee has already paid a membership contribution to the Flemish care fund once, then the enrollee can only still change care funds via the procedure described in chapter V.

### Section III.—Multiple enrollments with different care funds in the same period

A person cannot be enrolled with different care funds. The Flemish Care Fund will check this by requesting the national register numbers of the enrolled members with the different care funds. As a principle, the person – who has paid his contribution – is enrolled with the care fund that was the first to notify the Flemish Care Fund within the deadlines provided for that purpose that the person is enrolled with it. If the care funds notify at the same time that a person is enrolled with them, then the person is enrolled with the care fund that was the first to receive the membership contribution of the enrollee. The other care funds inform the person in writing that his enrollment with them lapses because he was already enrolled with a care fund. If the person has obtained multiple coverages with different care funds in the same period, then the care funds – with the exception of the care fund with which the person is effectively enrolled – reclaim the amounts from this person.

### Section IV.—Termination of enrollment

An enrollment can only be terminated for the following reasons: +the enrollee leaves Flanders or Brussels

+the enrollee dies +the enrollee residing in Brussels terminates his enrollment on a voluntary basis The enrollment is terminated on December 31 of the year in which the reason for termination of the enrollment occurred. The enrollee or his representative notifies the care fund by letter that the enrollment is being terminated. In case of death, the representative of the user attaches a copy of the death certificate. The enrollee must in principle still pay the full membership contribution for the year in which the reason for termination of the enrollment occurred. By way of derogation from this, in the event of death of the enrollee, the membership contributions need no longer be paid if the enrollee would still owe them.

If an enrollee leaves Flanders or Brussels, the care fund provides the following data of the user to the Flemish Care Fund within six months after the termination of the enrollment of the user: +the data of the enrollee that are kept in the register +an overview of the payments of all membership contributions of the enrollee +where applicable, an overview of the coverages of the last year

## CHAPTER IV.—Sanctions for non-enrollment and non-payment

When a person does not pay his membership contribution, the following sanctions apply: *the suspension of coverage with loss of rights The care fund must suspend the user's right to coverage for four months for each year that the user has not paid, although he was obliged to do so in that year. One is obliged to pay for a year in the following cases: (cid:226) if one is enrolled with a care fund on the first of January of the year in question; (cid:226) if one is enrolled ex officio as of that date in the year in question. This suspension takes effect at the moment the enrollee as user is entitled to coverage within the framework of the care insurance (i.e., the first day of the third month following the submission of an application). The user also loses his rights to coverage for those suspended periods. There is both a suspension and a waiting period. If a user has incurred both a suspension and a waiting period, then these periods are cumulated.

1.suspension The care fund must suspend the user's right to coverage for four months for each year that the user has not paid, although he was enrolled with a care fund. Non-payment of the contribution for the period from October 1, 2001 to and including December 31, 2002 leads to a suspension of four months.

2.waiting period The care fund must suspend the user's right to coverage for four months for each year that the user lived in Brussels and has not enrolled with a care fund, although he could have enrolled. Not being enrolled during the period from October 1, 2001 to and including December 31, 2002 leads to a waiting period of four months.

The care fund registers the suspension/or waiting period when the contribution for a given year is not paid. +When an enrollee who submits an application for coverage within the framework of the care insurance does not pay his membership contribution in the first quarter of the year, the amount of the contribution is deducted from the amount of the coverage from the month of April in the month in which the execution of the coverage starts. +When an enrollee who already has the right to coverage does not pay his membership contribution in the first quarter of the year, the amount of the contribution is deducted from the amount of the coverage in the month of April.

## CHAPTER V.—Change of care fund

A change of care fund is legally valid if: + the member has paid his membership contribution to the new care fund in the first quarter of the year that he, after his application for transfer, can be registered with the new care fund

+ the new care fund has received an overview of all payments of the membership contributions and an overview of the coverage of the last year from the old care fund. For this purpose, the following procedure is followed: + the member notifies before 5 December of the calendar year preceding that on which he will be registered with his new care fund, to this new care fund that he wishes to join it. + the new care fund requests no later than 15 December of the calendar year preceding that on which the person will be registered with the new care fund, an overview of all payments of the membership contributions and an overview of the coverage, carried out during the last year.

+ the old care fund provides before 15 January of the calendar year in which the person concerned will join the new care fund, the following data: (cid:226) the data of the user that must be included in the register (national register number, name, address, date of birth, sex, nationality); (cid:226) an overview of the payments of the membership contributions copy of the last decision of the old care fund regarding coverage of the member (only if the member is entitled to coverage); (cid:226) the overview of the coverage, carried out during the last year + the old care fund terminates the membership of the member concerned on 31 December of the year in which the new care fund has requested the overviews within the specified period + the candidate member is obliged to pay his annual membership contribution to the new care fund in the first quarter of the year in which he can join his new care fund. + the registration takes retroactive effect from 1 January of the year in which the member paid his contribution to the new care fund for the first time. If the user has not paid his due membership contribution to the new care fund before 1 April, then the registration with the new care fund lapses. The user is then registered again with the old care fund. The new care fund informs the old care fund within 40 days that the transfer lapses and that the person is registered again with the old care fund. Coverage is paid out by the care fund with which the user was registered in the month to which the coverage relates. This means that no credit transfer from the old to the new care fund will take place. Recoveries from the user are also carried out by the care fund with which the user was registered at the time that the coverage, to which the recovery relates, was wrongly executed.

### Section II.—Users who are older than 25 years

Subsection I.—General

The user or his representative submits an application for coverage to his care fund via the application form in the context of the Flemish care insurance. A person cannot submit an application for coverage if he is not registered with the care fund by paying at least one contribution. The user or his representative completes the application form in the context of the Flemish care insurance, signs it and provides it to his care fund. The user must meet the following formal conditions at the time of his application for coverage:

Subsection II.—Formal conditions for coverage for users who live in Flanders 1. have resided uninterruptedly in Flanders or Brussels for the last 5 years; Belgians and nationals of another member state of the European Union do not fall under this condition if they still pay 5 times the amount of the annual contribution at the time of the application for coverage 2. have paid the contribution for the current year before 1 April or before submitting the application 3. have not submitted an application for coverage to another care fund in the current year 4. as regards the applications for coverage for residential care, actually reside in a recognized or with a recognition equivalent residential facility

Subsection III.—Formal conditions for coverage for users who live in Brussels

1. The resident of Brussels who registers late, is registered uninterruptedly with a recognized care fund for a period determined by the government, prior to the date on which he submits his application for coverage.

Persons register late when they: + either do not register within 6 months in the calendar year in which they turn 26 years

+ or do not register within 6 months after they come to live in Brussels in a calendar year in which they turn at least 26 years. 2002 0 years

The care fund verifies whether the user meets the formal conditions and requests, if necessary, further evidence than the statement of the user or his representative on the application form in the context of the care insurance. If the user does not meet the formal conditions, then the care fund communicates that decision in writing to the user or his representative with clarification of the reason.

If the user meets the formal conditions, then it is checked whether a completed indication or a certificate is available.

Subsection IV.—Choice of type of care

There are two possibilities:

+ home and community care;

+ residential care. Only persons who reside in a home environment can submit an application for home and community care. The home environment is the place where the user actually lives or resides, excluding collective forms of housing where persons reside and are housed in a durable manner. A service flat is considered a home environment.

Only persons who reside in a recognized or with a recognition equivalent residential facility can submit an application for residential care.

### Section II.—User or their representative has no certificate

The user or their representative, who has no certificate, must have a new indication assessment carried out by an authorised indication assessor. At the request of the user or their representative, the health insurance fund makes available a list of all authorised indication assessors from the region to the user or their representative. If the user so wishes, the indication assessment takes place at the location where the user resides. The user is assessed by the authorised indication assessor using the BEL profile scale of the care insurance. The indication assessor completes the CHAPTER "Establishment of reduced self-care capacity". The user's BEL photograph is completed using the BEL guidelines. It is signed by the person carrying out the indication assessment. The user receives the indication assessment and submits the indication assessment to the health insurance fund. The user must score at least 35 points on the BEL profile scale to be considered as suffering from a serious and long-term reduced self-care capacity for interventions and services for home care and assistance.

EXCEPTIONS

The assessment for users between five and eighteen years of age shall be carried out using an adapted BEL scale (BEL-children). The user must score at least 10 points on the physical A.D.L. block of the BEL profile scale to be considered as suffering from a serious and long-term reduced self-care capacity.

Users under five years of age cannot have an indication assessment carried out using the BEL profile scale of the care insurance.

## CHAPTER IX.—Compensation for carrying out the indication assessment

The Flemish Care Fund pays the health insurance fund 75 euros per indication assessment carried out, provided that + it concerns an indication assessment that was carried out within the framework of the care insurance by an authorised indication assessor at the request of the user or their representative (this therefore concerns the indication assessments that serve as a second option alongside the certificates) + the health insurance fund compensates the authorised indication assessor for the indication assessment

+ it does not concern an indication assessment that was carried out by a recognised family care service or a PCSW that has a recognised family care service, whereby family care was provided to the assessed user within six months after carrying out the indication assessment by this facility. Of each indication assessment of a user, which was carried out within the framework of the care insurance, the authorised indication assessors provide a copy of the BEL photograph (front and back) to the health insurance fund to which the user is affiliated.

The family care service or the PCSW that has a recognised family care service informs the health insurance fund, where applicable, that it has provided family care to the user within six months after carrying out the indication assessment. This notification takes place within thirty days after the facts. The health insurance funds pay 75 euros per indication assessment carried out that meets the above-mentioned conditions to the authorised indication assessors.

Monthly, the health insurance funds submit an application to the Flemish Care Fund. This application contains the number of indication assessments carried out that meet the above-mentioned conditions. The Care Fund compensates the health insurance funds monthly. The Care Fund may draw up further instructions for this purpose.

If an indication assessment, for which the health insurance fund has already received compensation, is no longer eligible for compensation, the health insurance fund states this in the next application. It submits a credit note to the Flemish Care Fund for this purpose. Where applicable, the compensation paid out for this is deducted from the compensation to be paid in the following month.

7076 MONITEUR BELGE — 12.02.2003 — BELGISCH STAATSBLAD

The application by the health insurance funds must meet the following formal requirements: + the application is drawn up in the form of a collective statement, on which all invoices are listed that were submitted during the past month by the authorised indication assessors; + per invoice, the following data are included on the collective statement: (cid:226) the name and GKB number of the authorised indication assessor; (cid:226) the invoice date; (cid:226) the number of invoiced indication assessments per indication assessor; (cid:226) the invoiced amount per indication assessor; (cid:226) where applicable: the number of reclaimed indication assessments per indication assessor; (cid:226) where applicable: the reclaimed amount per indication assessor; (cid:226) where applicable: the difference between the invoiced and the reclaimed amount per indication assessor; + the collective statement states the total amount for which reimbursement is requested. This total amount is the difference between the total of all invoiced amounts and all reclaimed amounts or credit notes + a copy of all invoices and any credit notes to which it relates is attached to the collective statement. + each health insurance fund may only submit one collective statement per month. This collective statement may possibly be divided into partial statements per department, but is always transmitted in one consignment to the Flemish Care Fund. Where applicable, the attached copies of invoices and credit notes are grouped per partial statement.

+ the collective statement must be submitted to the Flemish Care Fund at the latest on the 15th day of the month following the month to which it relates.

The Flemish Care Fund has the right to transfer the collective statement that has not been submitted on time to the next payment date.

## CHAPTER X.—Guide BEL care insurance

BEL-PHOTO-USER

I. Who what and how is scored?

1) WHO

We always score the user, for whom the coverage in the framework of the care insurance was requested.

2) WHAT AND HOW?

We assess the care needs of the user by means of scoring the person in the broad sense of the word. We make a photo of the user via 27 items. These 27 items are categorized in blocks that each refer to a specific angle.

We distinguish 4 blocks.

BLOCK A: HOUSEHOLD A.D.L.

This is the first facet of our photo. This block refers to household assistance. In the gradation of loss of functions in the user, those of block A will be the first to fail and/or where the care need becomes apparent.

In block A the care need can be at different levels:

+ physically unable

+ not willing to do, psychologically unable

+ not learned.

BLOCK B: PHYSICAL A.D.L.

This facet of the photo refers to physical functions that fail. This is the block that is analogous with home nursing, where the gradation of loss of physical functions is assessed. This part of the photo provides information about the care needs of the user at physical bodily level. Here links are made to needs at the level of body care, hygienic and nursing care. In block B the care need is interpreted in a strict sense; namely physically unable. Block A and B form 1 type of dimension, 1 fairly coherent whole, refer to material tasks. Both blocks follow the line of thinking: a loss of functions.

BLOCK C: SOCIAL A.D.L.

In this block the care need for social support for certain facets is assessed.

This block makes links to concrete tasks at the level of general and family assistance. To know whether a user needs this type of assistance, we need to have a photo of the care need for support. This can be at the social-relational level (support in processing loss, the social consequences for the user e.g.) as well as at the preventive level (HEP) as at the guidance level (childcare, adherence to therapy and health rules, financial transactions,... ). This block provides additional information about the extent to which a user is socially integrated, maintains himself socially. In block C the care need as in A can be at different levels: at physical, psychological and social level. Block C is of a different nature than A and B. The care need that is expressed here, will also lead to care tasks, but rather the less visible tasks, the tasks that are too often not named, or get too little recognition. Working on self-reliance will often constitute an important part of the concrete care provision here. E.g. socially vulnerable families, processing of social problems, rehabilitation after accident. In the elderly the concrete situations will more often concern «social A.D.L.» and in families it will often be about assistance with parenthood, this must be kept in mind when scoring. We therefore translate 5 items in care needs towards the elderly, and 2 items, specifically towards families.

MONITEUR BELGE — 12.02.2003 — BELGISCH STAATSBLAD 7077

BLOCK D: MENTAL HEALTH.

This block refers to psychological dysfunction. This block provides additional and supplementary information to the other blocks.

In block D the care need is viewed at different levels, besides psychological dysfunctions, external behaviors and moods are also scored. This care need is translated into a degree of occurrence.

The facets of the photo that we want to obtain here are data and visible signs of the extent to which the user can maintain and function psychologically independently and autonomously. II. The principles and rules of scoring in the B.EL. photo of the user

1) METHOD OF SCORING: principles and motives.

Four blocks therefore each with 6 to 8 items. 1. Each item is scored separately but within the philosophy of the block to which it belongs.

2. Each block (and item) is equally structured. This means: there are always 4 gradation levels with each time the same value structure.

For each item the applicable level is marked. In case of doubt between 2 levels one takes the highest (most care needing) level.

3. We use everywhere the SUM score, i.e. in each block sum all scores. The intermediate scores form a total score. 4. We make a photo of the user. I.e. we assess the degree of care need of the user. 5. For blocks A, B and C, this is expressed in:

LEVEL 0 NO CARE NEED

LEVEL 1 SOME CARE NEED

LEVEL 2 MORE CARE NEED

LEVEL 3 COMPLETE CARE NEED

For block D this is expressed in a DEGREE OF OCCURRENCE. In a further paragraph about the concrete application rules these levels of care need are translated into a level at which the user still functions independently and needs help. Here deliberately no link is made to tasks. Tasks can receive a derivative in a separate section, separate from the care need. 6. When scoring an item, no account may be taken of the fact that the user already appeals or will appeal to a certain help (informal care, professional help,...). 7. When scoring an item, there does not necessarily have to be a correlation with:

+ the cause of the need for help

+ the formulated help request of the help seeker

2) DESIGN OF THE RULES: principles

The principles.

A short manual with level indications that are general but unambiguous

+ return consistently

+ are understood by everyone from the same language, but per scoring must be considered or interpreted

+ contains no examples (unless as illustration during training)

4) THE DICTIONARY OF THE ITEMS.

Block A:

MAINTENANCE

= maintenance of the dwelling in all its aspects ranging from tidying up to tasks requiring endurance and strength.

WASHING

= ranging from collecting and sorting the laundry to and including drying and/or taking down from the clothesline.

IRONING

= ranging from folding to and including storing in the cupboard.

SHOPPING

= ranging from making the shopping list to and including putting away the purchased products.

PREPARING MEALS

= ranging from drawing up the menu to and including serving the prepared meal.

ORGANIZATION OF HOUSEHOLD WORK

= managing a household, ranging from planning all household work that must be done regularly or sporadically to and including monitoring its execution.

Block B

WASHING

= taking care of one's own body hygiene.

DRESSING

= being able to dress and undress oneself.

MOVING

= being able to move oneself, indoors or outdoors.

TOILET VISIT

= going to the toilet and making appropriate use of the toilet.

INCONTINENCE

= not feeling the urge to, not being able to control the discharge of urine or faeces

EATING

= one's own food intake: ranging from using the cutlery oneself to and including bringing the food to the mouth.

Block C:

SOCIAL LOSS

= receiving, processing the social consequences of loss, with regard to:

* loss of familiar person

* loss of familiar environment

* loss of work, status

* loss of physical or mental health

* loss of..

ADHERENCE TO THERAPY AND HEALTH RULES

complying with, following common sense rules or prescribed rules with regard to:

* medication use

* therapy

* rehabilitation

* lifestyle rules

* diets

* prevention of complications

7080 MONITEUR BELGE — 12.02.2003 — BELGISCH STAATSBLAD

SAFETY IN AND AROUND THE HOME

= having insight into and eliminating safety risks in and around the home.

ADMINISTRATION

= ranging from following up on correspondence, writing letters, filling in forms to visiting the post office and contacts with all kinds of agencies.

FINANCIAL TRANSACTIONS

= executing payments and financial assignments

HYGIENIC CARE OF CHILDREN

= depending on the age of the children: ranging from washing, changing, dressing and feeding to guiding and supervising the hygienic care by the parent(s) or the child(ren) themselves.

CHILDCARE

= depending on the age of the children: ranging from watching the children to guiding and supervising the time spending of the child(ren)

Block D:

DISORIENTATION IN TIME

= the extent to which the user no longer has correct insight into time. Insight into time includes: + correctly indicating the time when asked. + using the time concept in an appropriate manner (hour, morning, afternoon, day, night, week, month, year).

DISORIENTATION IN SPACE

= the extent to which the user no longer finds his way indoors or outdoors E.g.: not finding the toilet, getting lost, believing to be in another house, wandering around,

DISORIENTATION IN PERSONS

= the extent to which the user no longer recognizes persons or no longer associates them with their correct name. E.g.: seeing daughter as own mother, seeing grandchildren as own children, addressing nursing staff with wrong name,...

NON-GOAL-DIRECTED BEHAVIOR

= the extent to which the user displays behavior that is not sensible, does not serve a purpose. This includes + compulsive actions (cleaning mania, fear of contamination, plucking at the blanket,...) + restlessness: psycho-motor agitation (flight behavior, pacing behavior,...)

DISTURBING BEHAVIOR

= the extent to which the user behaves disturbingly in his social environment. This includes all social interaction problems: + verbally disturbing (calling, shouting,)

+ improper (undressing, spitting, urinating, masturbating, in public) + destructive violence towards persons, destructiveness.

LACKING INITIATIVE BEHAVIOR

= the extent to which the user shows lack of initiative and does not come to action from himself. This includes: + no action without stimulus

+ difficulty in starting activities + slow (no appropriate speed) in executing activities.

DEPRESSED MOOD

= the extent to which the user displays a depressed mood (ranging from tendency to cry to depression) or expresses suffering from it.

ANXIOUS MOOD

= the extent to which the user shows anxiety or expresses suffering from it (ranging from worrying about small things to insomnia, to no longer functioning adequately due to anxiety). III. The duration of the serious, reduced self-care capacity After calculating the total score the indicator must also make an estimate of the duration of the serious, reduced self-care capacity, i.e. how long will the user suffer from this degree of care dependence? This is always a subjective assessment, which will be based on the observation of the user, but also on previous experiences.

## CHAPTER XI.—Decision on coverage

The care fund decides on the application for coverage and determines the validity period of the decision based on the indication assessment or the certificate. The care fund may conduct or have conducted additional examinations with regard to the indication assessment before deciding on the coverage. However, these additional examinations are limited to a review of the original indication assessment. These examinations may be conducted by a person appointed by the care fund or by an authorized indicator whom the care fund designates for this purpose. This indicator must be a different person than the one who carried out the last indication assessment of the user.

The person who carries out the indication assessment in the context of an additional examination must at least have the level of the degree of graduate in family sciences, graduate in the field of study healthcare or the field of study social educational work, or have a diploma that is recognized as equivalent by the Department of Education of the Ministry of the Flemish Community both in terms of content and level.

MONITEUR BELGE — 12.02.2003 — BELGISCH STAATSBLAD 7081

The examination must always be conducted using the BEL profile scale of the care insurance. The BEL photo user is completed. If the user wishes, the indication assessment is carried out at the place where the user resides. After carrying out the indication assessment, the indicator sends the completed form 'additional examination or review concerning the determination of long-term, seriously reduced self-care capacity' to the care fund. The care fund may, on the basis of this additional examination, contest the severity and duration of the reduced self-care capacity. In that case, it notifies the user or his representative and also notifies that the user or his representative have the right to be heard. If the user or his representative requests this, they are heard by the care fund before it makes its final decision. The validity period of the positive decision of the care fund is determined in accordance with the chapter 'validity period of a decision'.

The care fund may refuse an application for coverage because the user is not considered to be in long-term and serious need of care. The care fund notifies the user or his representative in writing of its decision on the coverage. This notification contains, in the case of a positive decision, at least the following information: + the indication that the user is eligible for the care insurance + the indication from when the user can exercise his right to coverage + the indication of the validity period of the decision for coverage + the indication of which forms of care for which amount the user can claim

+ a justification of the decision + the notification that the user or his representative has the right to request a review of the decision on coverage at any time

+ the notice that the user or his representative must immediately notify his care fund of a change in the form of care and any (serious) change in the health condition of the user. It is added that failure to notify these changes may lead to the recovery from a user of wrongly obtained coverage

+ where applicable, the period of suspension of the coverage. This notification contains, in the case of a negative decision, the following information: + the notice that the user is not eligible for the care insurance + the indication of the reason or reasons why the user is not eligible + a copy of the application and the indication assessments + the notice that the user or his representative may lodge an objection against the decision of the care fund with the Flemish Care Fund and a description of all modalities regarding the lodging of an objection and the procedure to be followed.

## CHAPTER XII.—Validity period of a decision

The care fund decides on the duration of the validity of a positive decision based on the indication assessment. A positive decision of a care fund is valid for at least six months.

The validity period of a positive decision for informal and home care is a maximum of three years, except for the validity period of a positive decision based on a certificate based on at least a B-score in home nursing for users who are younger than 75 years at the time the decision takes effect, where the maximum validity period is one year. The validity period of a positive decision for residential care is valid for an indefinite period. The validity period of the positive decision of the care fund is calculated from the day on which the decision for coverage takes effect.

## CHAPTERXIII.—Changeofcareform

A change of care form occurs when one switches from informal and home care to professional residential care recognized within the framework of care insurance or equivalent to recognition (and vice versa). The user or their representative must immediately request any change in the choice of a specific care form from the care fund.

The care fund decides on the application for change of care form. The care fund communicates its decision in writing to the user or their representative. This communication contains at least the following information in case of a positive decision: +the indication that the user is eligible for the change of care form +the indication from when the user is entitled to coverage for the new care form +the indication of which care forms and which amounts the user can claim +the indication of the validity period of the decision This communication contains at least the following information in case of a negative decision: +the notification that the user is not eligible for the change of care form +the indication of the reason or reasons why the user is not eligible

7082 MONITEUR BELGE — 12.02.2003 — BELGISCH STAATSBLAD

In case of change from informal and home care to residential care, the coverage for the new care form, possibly with retroactive effect, takes effect from the first day of the first full month that the user stays in a recognized or equivalent residential facility.

In case of change from residential care to informal and home care, the coverage for the new care form, possibly with retroactive effect, takes effect from the first day of the month following the one in which the user left the recognized or equivalent residential facility.

A move from a recognized or equivalent residential facility to another residential facility cannot be considered as a change of care form. This must be handled via an application for revision.

Transitional measure For users who change from informal and home care to residential care before 1 April 2003 and who have a B-score on the evaluation scale in support of the application for an allowance in a care institution, the coverage for residential care is carried out from 1 April 2003.
