## CHAPTERXIV.—Decisiontorevise,extendorterminatean(original)positivedecision

### SectionI.—Revisionatrequestoftheuserortheirrepresentative

The user or their representative has the right to request a revision at any time. The revision is done by completing a revision form. The user or their representative completes the form, signs it and submits it to their care fund.

The procedure regarding verification of the formal conditions and the possible determination of the reduced self-care capacity proceeds in accordance with the chapter on handling an application. The care fund decides on the revision of the coverage. The care fund communicates its decision on the revision of the coverage in writing to the user or their representative within 60 days after the application was submitted. This communication contains, in case of a positive decision, at least the information that is communicated in case of a positive decision on an application for coverage. The positive decision on the revision of the coverage takes effect from the first day of the month following the date on which the revision was requested by the user or their representative.

A move from a recognized or equivalent residential facility to another residential facility must be handled via an application for revision.

MONITEUR BELGE — 12.02.2003 — BELGISCH STAATSBLAD 7083

### SectionII.—Extensionofapositivedecision

The care fund communicates in writing to the user or their representative three months before the expiry of the validity period that they must submit an application for extension of the positive decision. The user, in consultation with the care fund, relies on a certificate or the user or their representative designates, in consultation with the care fund, an authorized indicator. The serious and long-term reduced self-care capacity is determined in accordance with chapter VIII.

The care fund decides on the extension and determines the validity period of the decision based on the indication or the certificate. The care fund may conduct or have conducted additional investigations regarding the indication before deciding on the coverage. The care fund takes a positive decision on the extension if the user is still considered to have long-term and serious care needs. Otherwise, the decision is terminated. The care fund communicates its decision on the extension of the coverage in writing to the user or their representative. This communication contains, in case of a positive decision, at least the information that is communicated in case of a positive decision on an application for coverage. The extension of the coverage takes effect on the day that the validity period of the previous decision expires.

### Section III.—Termination of a positive decision

The care fund terminates a decision if the user no longer meets the formal conditions or if the user is no longer considered seriously and chronically in need of care. The care fund communicates the decision to terminate in writing to the user or their representative. The communication contains the following information in case of termination: +the notification that the user is no longer eligible for care insurance +the notification of the date from which the user will no longer receive amounts +the statement of the reason or reasons why the user is no longer eligible +where applicable, a copy of the determination of long-term, seriously reduced self-care capacity +the notification that the user or their representative can submit an objection against the decision of the care fund to the Flemish Care Fund and a description of all modalities relating to the submission of an objection and the procedure to be followed. The decision takes effect on the first day of the month following the day on which the user no longer meets the formal conditions or on which the user is no longer considered to have long-term and seriously reduced self-care capacity. The decision to terminate as a result of no longer being considered to have long-term and seriously reduced self-care capacity takes effect: +on the first day of the month after the decision to terminate by the care fund in case of a decision for informal and home care

+ on the first day of the month after the determination by the insurance institution, in case of a decision for residential care

## CHAPTER XV.—Control of coverage

Additional investigations and controls are carried out on at least 10% of the initial applications for coverage, where an indication assessment has been carried out by an authorized indication assessor or where a certificate has been issued by a family care service and where the application has not been refused on the basis of the formal conditions. The care fund can verify the indication assessment of the user receiving coverage. These are controls after the care fund has already taken a decision on the coverage. The care fund verifies on its own initiative or at the instruction of the Flemish Care Fund the indication assessment. The control is carried out by a person appointed by the care fund or by an authorized indication assessor designated by the care fund for this purpose. This indication assessor must be different from the one who carried out the last indication assessment of the user. The person carrying out the indication assessment must at least have the level of the degree of graduate in family sciences, graduate in the study area of health care or the study area of social and educational work, or a diploma that is equated with this in terms of both content and level by the Education Department of the Ministry of the Flemish Community.

The control must always be carried out using the BEL profile scale of the care insurance. The BEL photo of the user is completed. If the user so wishes, then the indication assessment is carried out at the place where the user resides. The indication assessor must carry out the indication assessment within twenty days after the day on which the care fund designated the indication assessor. This date also determines the order in which the indication assessments of different users must be provided to the care fund by the same indication assessor. After carrying out the indication assessment, the indication assessor sends the completed form 'additional investigation or control concerning the determination of long-term, seriously reduced self-care capacity' to the care fund. The care fund decides on the control of coverage and determines the validity period of the decision based on the indication assessment. The care fund can refuse an application for coverage because the user is no longer considered chronically and seriously in need of care. The care fund communicates in writing to the user or their representative its decision on coverage within sixty days after the notification to the user or their representative that a new indication assessment will be carried out. This communication contains at least the information communicated in case of a positive decision on an application for coverage in case of a positive decision.

7084 MONITEUR BELGE — 12.02.2003 — BELGISCH STAATSBLAD

The communication contains the following information in case of a negative decision: +the notification that the user is no longer eligible for care insurance +the notification of the date from which the user will no longer receive amounts +the statement of the reason or reasons why the user is no longer eligible

+ a copy of the form 'additional investigation or control concerning the determination of long-term, seriously reduced self-care capacity' +the notification that the user or their representative can submit an objection against the decision of the care fund to the Flemish Care Fund and a description of all modalities relating to the submission of an objection and the procedure to be followed. The decision as a result of the control of coverage takes effect from the first day of the month following the date of decision. If the control takes place at the instruction of the Care Fund, then the care fund also informs the Care Fund of the decision.

## CHAPTER XVI.—Implementation of coverage

### Section I.—Financial arrangement: general

The right to coverage commences on the first day of the third month after the application. Any suspension of coverage (see chapter IV) also commences from the first day of the third month after the application. The user receives the following amount for coverage of their costs depending on their choice of a particular form of care: form of care amount per month informal and home care 90 euros professional residential care 125 euros

In case of a user who dies in the course of the month, the coverage for the month in question is still implemented. Persons residing in a recognized or with recognition equated rest home or rest and nursing home receive coverage until 31 March 2003 only if they meet the formal conditions and if they have a C score on the scale for intervention in a care institution. If such a person would have a B score after recalculation, then the coverage for residential care is implemented again from 1 April 2003. The user or their representative does not need to submit a new application for this.

### Section II.—Financial arrangement regarding informal and home care for users residing in Flanders

The care fund pays the amount for informal and home care to the user monthly in cash to the user or by transfer to the user's account number.

### Section III.—Financial arrangement regarding informal and home care for users residing in Brussels

The care fund pays the amount for informal and home care to the user in cash to the user or by transfer to the user's account number. The coverage for informal and home care is only implemented if the user in the same month makes use of or resides in one of the following facilities recognized by the Flemish Government or equated with recognition:

+a family care service

+a local or regional service center

+a short-stay center

+a day care center

+a respite care service

+a service flat

+ a family care service or PCSW that provides cleaning assistance recognized or equated with recognition in the framework of care insurance.

If such a facility provides care to the user in a particular month, then the user is entitled to coverage for informal and home care for that month.

The invoice that the user provides to the care fund serves as proof of the provision of care. The invoice must be submitted within six months after the service month to which it relates. The invoice contains the following information:

+invoicing date and invoice number

+name and address of facility or GKB number of facility

+name, first name and address of user +period (at least per calendar month) in which service was provided +designation of type of assistance in accordance with recognition (for example: non-logistical assistance but cleaning assistance)

MONITEUR BELGE — 12.02.2003 — BELGISCH STAATSBLAD 7085

### Section IV.—Financial arrangements regarding services provided by professional, residential facilities

The care fund pays the coverage for residential care directly to the user in cash or via transfer to the user's account number. This is done on the basis of a valid certificate based on a score that qualifies for residential care in accordance with the chapter 'determination of serious and long-term reduced self-care capacity at the application for coverage'. The user or their representative provides the care fund with the said certificate. The residential facility reports the following data to the care fund in the month following the one in which the user's situation in these areas has changed: +termination of the residence agreement with the residential facility by the user; +death of the user;

+ change in the score on the assessment scale in support of the application for a contribution in a care institution whereby the user no longer falls under the conditions in the area of care needs within the framework of care insurance.

The residential facility reports this by the fifth day of the month following the one in which the user's situation has changed. Payment takes place at the earliest during the month following the month to which the coverage relates.

## CHAPTER XVII.—Recovery of unduly executed coverages

### Section I.—Recovery

The care fund can recover coverages that it has wrongly executed for a user in two ways: +it deducts the amounts from the coverages that it will execute for the user in the following months

+it recovers the amounts from the user Before the care fund decides to recover the unduly executed coverages, it notifies the user or their representative of this and also notifies them that the user or their representative has the right to be heard. If the user or their representative requests this, they will be heard by the care fund before it makes its final decision. The care fund communicates its decision in writing to the user. This communication contains at least the following information:

+the amount being recovered

+the reason or reasons why the amount is being recovered +the manner in which the amount will be recovered, including, where applicable, the repayment plan If the wrongful execution of the coverages is due to information deliberately withheld by the user or their representative or information deliberately provided incorrectly or incompletely, the care fund may suspend the execution of the coverage for a maximum of six months. The care fund communicates its decision in the letter announcing the recovery of the amount or amounts. The suspension takes effect on the first day of the month following the notification. Months in which the care fund deducts the coverage to recover amounts cannot count towards the suspension. The user or their representative may appeal to the Flemish Care Fund against the care fund's decision to suspend the user. The appeal does not lead to a suspension of the care fund's decision.

The limitation period for recovering unduly executed coverages is 150 days if the user proves that they received coverages in good faith to which they were not entitled. In other cases, the thirty-year limitation period applies.

### Section II.—Exemption from recovery

The care funds may be exempted by the Flemish Care Fund from recovery of unlawfully executed coverages from a user under the following conditions:

+ the undue payment must not result from an error, mistake or negligence of the care fund;

+ the care fund demonstrates that all means at its disposal, including legal remedies, have been used to claim repayment. This condition is considered fulfilled when the recovery is considered uncertain or when the costs associated with the execution of the recovery exceed the amount to be recovered. Sending a bailiff is not required if the amount to be recovered is less than 270 euros; +the undue payment of a coverage for informal and home care must not have occurred before the twentieth of the month to which it relates. The care fund requests the exemption from recovery from the Flemish Care Fund. The Fund designs a form for this purpose. The head official of the Flemish Care Fund makes a decision whether or not to exempt the care fund from the recovery of the unduly executed coverages. A negative decision by the head official is reasoned. The Care Fund communicates this decision to the care fund. The Flemish Care Fund may determine further rules regarding the exemption from recovery.

7086 MONITEUR BELGE — 12.02.2003 — BELGISCH STAATSBLAD

## CHAPTER XVIII.—Cumulation of the coverage with benefits under other provisions

### Section I.—Informal and home care and personal assistance budget (PAB)

Persons have no right to the execution of a coverage for informal and home care if in the same month they use the personal assistance budget (PAB). However, the decision cannot be refused or terminated because someone uses the personal assistance budget. The Flemish Fund for Social Integration of Persons with Disabilities regularly provides an updated file to the Flemish Care Fund. This file contains the following data of people who have a PAB: +national register number

+name and address of the disabled person

+PAB start date The Care Fund informs the care funds which of their affiliated persons are entitled to a PAB. They then have no right to informal and home care.

### Section II.—Informal and home care and residence in a residential facility recognized

by the Flemish Fund for Social Integration of Persons with Disabilities

Persons have no right to a coverage for informal and home care if, according to the protocol, they reside in a full-time regime in a residential facility recognized by the Flemish Fund for Social Integration of Persons with Disabilities. These are the following facilities:

+homes for non-workers

+boarding schools

+homes for workers

+services for sheltered living +centers for observation, orientation and medical, psychological and educational treatment In applicable cases, the coverages for the months in question are suspended. If a person who resides in a full-time regime in a residential facility recognized by the Flemish Fund according to a protocol is absent from this facility for 21 or more consecutive days, they are entitled to a coverage for informal and home care for the month or months in which this continuous period of absence falls.

The user or their representative must declare on the application form whether the user resides in a full-time regime in a residential facility recognized by the Flemish Fund or is on the waiting list for such a facility.

If the user only starts residing in such a facility after submitting their application form, they (or their representative) must report this to the care fund.

## CHAPTERXX.—Correction by the care funds of erroneous decisions

If the care fund itself has made an erroneous decision, they can correct this by sending a new decision to the user or their representative, whereby the old decision is declared null and void with mention of the reason why. If the correction of the erroneous decision occurs within sixty days of the request for (the revision of) the coverage, copies of the erroneous and corrected decisions must be kept in the file. If the erroneous decision is corrected later than sixty days after the request for (the revision of) the coverage, then in addition to keeping the copies in the files, copies of the erroneous and corrected decision must also be provided to the Flemish Care Fund.

Transition 2002-2003:

Members who terminate or have terminated their membership on 31/12/2002 may no longer be included in text file A, except for outgoing transfers from 2002 - 2003. These must still be included in files 200303A and 200306A.

Explanation of fields: national register number: national register number of the member concerned year of birth: year of birth of the member concerned municipality code: NIS code of the merged municipality where the person concerned last resided in the Flemish Region or the Brussels-Capital Region (situation last day of the requested quarter). date of commencement of membership: date of the membership of the member concerned with the care fund concerned. The membership date is always of the form 01/01/YYYY with YYYY the first year that the member concerned was affiliated with the care fund. date of payment of the last member contribution: date on which the last member contribution was paid by the member concerned. If a regularization is still paid by the member concerned in the year concerned, this field must not be adjusted. processing date of the commencement of membership membership code: the type of membership concerned 00=membership at start of care insurance. From next year this code may no longer be used for new memberships (except for persons who do not wish to join the Flemish care fund ex officio and still choose a care fund other than 680). 10=reaching age. This concerns persons who turn 26 in the current year and live in Flanders or in the Brussels-Capital Region. The member contribution must be paid before 1 July. 20=this code must be used when a person younger than 26 years submits an application for coverage.

21= this code must be used when a person younger than 26 years has a positive decision for coverage. 30=coming to live in Flanders. This concerns persons who turn 26 in the current year or are already and come to live in Flanders. The contribution must be paid within six months of coming to live in Flanders. 40=coming to live in Brussels. This concerns persons who turn 26 in the current year or are already and come to live in Brussels. The contribution must be paid within six months of coming to live in Brussels 50=voluntary membership in Brussels

7x=incoming transfer from care fund x 80=ex officio membership by the Flemish care fund. The Flemish care fund is the only care fund that may use this code.

0000=no termination of membership

Frequency and volume: The database data must be transferred quarterly. The database data are cumulative: the data from previous months must also be added. Except where explicitly stated, fields that do not need to be completed must be filled with zeros.

Transition 2002-2003: Given that the credits of 2002 may still be used until April 2003 inclusive, this means that all active files of 2002 must be included in the text file B of 2003.

Active files of the year 2002 are: —ongoing files (files whose end date of reimbursement falls after 31/12/2002) —files that are no longer ongoing but whose credit differs from zero These records remain in the text file B of 2003 at least until the end of 2003. The credit of 2002 can no longer be used after April 2003. This means that in files 200303B and 200306B the credit of 2002 is still included. After that the credit of 2002 is set to zero and thus it is no longer shared in files 200309B and 200312B. For Brussels it is such that there must still be «credit» for the year 2003 to be shared. This means that in files 200303B and 200306B the credit field for the files in Brussels will contain both the credit of 2002 and that of 2003.

Explanation of fields: a. national register number: b. year of birth:

- c. NIS-code merged municipality: NIS - code of the merged municipality where the person concerned was last resident in the Flemish Region or the Brussels-Capital Region (situation last day of the requested quarter)
- d. Code decentralized point of the health fund: the department where the file concerned can be physically consulted. e. Code application, review or extension: this field indicates whether this concerns an application, a review or an extension. This field was introduced since a review and an extension become a new record.
- f. date of the application, review or extension: date on which the application or review is requested. In case of an extension, this date is equal to the date of the execution of the indication or the date of the submission of the certificate to the health fund. g. code indicator or certificate with application, review or extension h. result of the indication with application, review or extension: here the score of the care need must be entered from the indication. If no indication has taken place (a certificate is used) then this field must be filled by entering «99», and not 00. So here «padding nines» are used instead of padding zeros. The score may be entered at the moment the health fund becomes aware of the result of the indication, thus before the actual decision was taken. i. code additional examination: here it is indicated whether an additional examination has taken place or not.
- j. date indication with additional examination: date on which the additional examination (thus actually the additional indication) took place k. result of the indication with additional examination: here the score of the indication must be entered with additional examination. The score may be entered at the moment the health fund becomes aware of the result of the indication. If no additional examination has taken place or the result is not yet known, then this field is filled by entering «99».

7092 MONITEUR BELGE — 12.02.2003 — BELGISCH STAATSBLAD

l. Code decision: is a merging of the previous fields j. «Code decision» and m. «code decision after additional examination». (The code here is the code with or without additional examination). The option 3 = additional examination in the previous field j. has been replaced by field i. «code additional examination».

m. date of the decision: date on which the decision on the application, review or extension was taken.

n. date last modification form of care: date on which the last modification of the form of care takes effect. If there are multiple modifications, the last date is retained. This date is the date on which the amount of the payment effectively changes. So it is always a date of the form 01/mm/yyyy. o. Code form of care: this field enters the last valid form of care. The code with the application no longer needs to be given. It is only if the application is approved that this field must be completed. In case of modification of the form of care, this field is simply adjusted and field n. «date last modification form of care» is entered. p. code decision after inspection: q. result of the indication with inspection: here the score must be entered of the indication with inspection. If no inspection has taken place or the result of the inspection is not yet known, then this field must be filled by entering 99, and not 00. So here «padding nines» are used instead of padding zeros. The score may be entered at the moment the health fund becomes aware of the result of the indication, this can thus fall before the date on which the actual decision of the inspection was taken. r. date of the decision after inspection: date on which the decision after inspection was taken. s. end date for execution of reimbursements: this field is the merging of fields n. «end date for execution of reimbursements» and x. «end date for execution of reimbursements after inspection or review» in the previous B-files. The last valid end date must be entered here. For residential care this date equals «00000000».

t. code payment or recovery: with this field we can determine whether field u. «amount paid or recovered during the calendar year concerned» is a positive or negative amount.

u. amount that was paid or recovered during the calendar year concerned: in this field the cumulative amount (or balance) of all payments and recoveries during the calendar year concerned is written. v. amount of the outstanding credit at the end of the quarter concerned fields w.; x.; y.; z.: are possibly still to be determined fields for the financial responsibility of the health funds

23DECEMBER2002.—Ministerialdecreeconcerningthedetermination of the forms in connection with care insurance The Flemish Minister of Welfare, Health, Equal Opportunities and Development Cooperation, Having regard to the decree of 30 March 1999 concerning the organisation of care insurance, as amended by the decrees of 22 December 1999, 8 December 2000, 18 May 2001 and 20 December 2002; Having regard to the decree of the Flemish Government of 28 September 2001 concerning the recognition, registration and authorisation, and concerning the affiliation, application and assumption of costs in the framework of care insurance, as amended by the decrees of the Flemish Government of 14 December 2001, 25 October 2002 and 13 December 2002; Having regard to the decree of the Flemish Government of 3 July 2002 determining the powers of the members of the Flemish Government, Decrees:

#### Article 1.

In execution of article 21, § 1, article 29, and article 34, § 1, of the decree of the Flemish Government of 28 September 2001 concerning the recognition, registration and authorisation, and concerning the affiliation, application and assumption of costs in the framework of care insurance, the user or his representative uses one of the following forms: 1˚ the application form annexed as Annex I to this decree, for an application for community and home care; 2˚ the application form annexed as Annex II to this decree, for an application for residential care.

#### Art. 2. In execution of article 37, § 1, of the same decree, the indication provider designated by the health insurance fund uses

the form annexed as Annex III to this decree.

#### Art. 3. The ministerial decree of 1 October 2001 concerning the determination of the forms in connection with

care insurance is repealed.

Welfare care

By decree of the Flemish Minister of Welfare, Health and Equal Opportunities of 17 January 2003 it is determined:

The non-profit association Centre for General Welfare Work Metropool in Antwerp is, with effect from 1 January 2003, recognised for fulfilling the tasks in accordance with the commitment that the centre has made in this regard in its policy plan that is valid for the period from 1 January 2003 up to and including 31 December 2005, including the additional task of social assistance and services to detainees and their next of kin with a view to their social reintegration. Pursuant to article 9 of the decree of the Flemish government of 12 October 2001 in implementation of the decree of

19 December 1997 concerning general welfare work, the minimum number of staff members for which the recognition applies is set at 78.25 full-time equivalents. The working area of this centre is determined as follows:

- administrative district of Antwerp, with the exception of the municipalities of Boom and Rumst (general tasks and the additional task of debt mediation); — judicial district of Antwerp (the additional task of perpetrator assistance). This recognition may be withdrawn at any moment for reasons of non-compliance with the conditions set by the regulations concerning the aforementioned organisation. This decree replaces the ministerial decree of 5 December 2002 concerning recognition of the non-profit association Centre for General Welfare Work Metropool in Antwerp. Welfare care By decree of the director-general of the administration Family and Social Welfare, on behalf of the Flemish minister responsible for assistance to persons, of 21 January 2003 it is determined: The P.C.S.W. of Evergem, Sleidinge-dorp 54, at 9940 Evergem, is granted prior authorisation for the construction of a service flat building with 5 housing units, Spoorwegstraat 12, at 9940 Evergem. This prior authorisation is valid for five years and this period of validity may be extended once by three years on condition that no later than two months before its expiry a receivable application for obtaining an extension of the granted prior authorisation is submitted to the administration. If the initiator has not commenced the works necessary to realise the initiative within the period of validity of the prior authorisation, the prior authorisation lapses by operation of law. If the initiative has been only partially realised twelve years after the date of the decision of the minister granting the prior authorisation, the prior authorisation lapses for the non-realised admission capacity. Any interested party may file with the Council of State by registered letter an application for suspension and/or an appeal for annulment against this decree within sixty days of notification of this decision. Department of Environment and Infrastructure

[C − 2003/35208]

Wastewater treatment infrastructure.—Declarations of public utility BILZEN AND HOESELT.—By decision of the Flemish Minister of Environment and Agriculture of 17 January 2003, the establishment of a wastewater treatment infrastructure is declared to be of public utility. Nr.20045

1) Geographic description:

Bilzen: collector Beverst phase 2. 2) Cadastral data:

City: Bilzen

Cadastrally known as: Division: 2; Section: F; Parcels: nos. 171e and 168e. Municipality: Hoeselt

Cadastrally known as: Division: 1; Section: B; Parcel: no. 162a. 3) Reason for declaration of public utility: For the establishment of the wastewater treatment infrastructure (collector) under, on or above private undeveloped land, which is not enclosed with a wall or a fence in accordance with the building or urban planning regulations. 4) Competent authority: Following this declaration of public utility, the N.V. Aquafin will be able to encumber the land with an easement or acquire it in the name of the Flemish Region. 5) Plans available for inspection at:

—MINISTRY OF THE FLEMISH COMMUNITY

Department of Environment and Infrastructure

Administration for Environmental, Nature, Land and Water Management

Water Division

Environmental Investments Unit

Alhambra

Emile Jacqmainlaan 20, box 5

1000 BRUSSELS

—N.V. Aquafin

Dijkstraat 8

2630 AARTSELAAR

—College of Burgomaster and Aldermen of and at

3740 BILZEN

—College of Burgomaster and Aldermen of and at

3730 HOESELT

6) Legal basis:

Decree of the Flemish Government dated 20 March 1991 establishing rules concerning the execution of works by N.V. Aquafin in application of articles 32septies and 32octies of the Act of 26 March 1971 on the protection of surface waters against pollution. 7) Date + competent minister

17 January 2003

The Flemish Minister of Environment and Agriculture, Vera DUA

8) Limitation Any interested party may, by means of a signed petition, lodge an appeal for annulment against this decision with the Council of State within a period of 60 days after becoming aware of it. The petition must be lodged by registered mail with the Council of State together with three certified copies and, in addition, as many copies as there are opposing parties (

#### article 85 of the rules of procedure of the

Council of State).

OFFICIAL NOTICES — OFFICIAL NOTICES

COURT OF ARBITRATION

Notice prescribed by article 74 of the special law of 6 January 1989 on the Court of Arbitration By application addressed to the Court by registered letter posted on 30 January 2003 and received at the registry on 31 January 2003, J. Van den Driessche, residing at 1700 Dilbeek, Baron de Vironlaan 80, box 25, has instituted an action for annulment and an application for suspension of article 5 of the law of 13 December 2002 amending the Electoral Code as well as its annex, and article 16 of the law of 13 December 2002 containing various amendments to electoral legislation (published in the Belgian Official Gazette of 10 January 2003), on grounds of violation of articles 10 and 11 of the Constitution.

This case is registered under number 2621 on the Court's roll.

The Registrar, L. Potoms.

COURT OF ARBITRATION

Notice prescribed by article 74 of the special law of 6 January 1989 on the Court of Arbitration

By application addressed to the Court by letter registered at the post office on 30 January 2003 and received at the registry on 31 January 2003, J. Van den Driessche, residing at 1700 Dilbeek, Baron de Vironlaan 80, box 25, has instituted an action for annulment and an application for suspension of article 5 of the law of 13 December 2002 modifying the Electoral Code as well as its annex and of article 16 of the law of 13 December 2002 containing various modifications regarding electoral legislation (published in the Belgian Official Gazette of 10 January 2003), on grounds of violation of articles 10 and 11 of the

Constitution.

This case is registered under number 2621 on the Court's roll.

The Registrar, L. Potoms.

COURT OF ARBITRATION

Notice prescribed by article 74 of the Special Law of 6 January 1989 on the Court of Arbitration By a writ addressed to the Court by registered letter posted on 30 January 2003 and received at the registry on 31 January 2003, J. Van den Driessche, residing at 1700 Dilbeek, Baron de Vironlaan 80 Bk. 25, raised an action for annulment and interim suspension of article 5 of the Law of 13 December 2002 amending the Electoral Code and its annex and of article 16 of the Law of

13 December 2002 establishing various amendments to electoral legislation (published in the Belgian Official Gazette of 10 January 2003), on grounds of violation of articles 10 and 11 of the Constitution. This case was registered under number 2621 in the Court's roll.

The Registrar, L. Potoms.

BELGIAN OFFICIAL GAZETTE — 12.02.2003 — BELGIAN OFFICIAL GAZETTE 7119

COURT OF ARBITRATION

Notice prescribed by article 74 of the special law of 6 January 1989 on the Court of Arbitration By judgment of 3 December 2002 in the case of the public prosecutor against C. Guinchon and Y. Fourmeau, of which the copy was received at the registry of the Court of Arbitration on 10 December 2002, the Court of Appeal of Mons has posed the following prejudicial question:

"Are articles 10 and 11 of the Constitution, as well as article 6 of the European Convention for the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms, violated by articles 67, §§ 1 and 2, of the CWATUP, decree of 14 May 1984, and 155, §§ 1 and 2, of the CWATUP, decree of 27 November 1997, which allow the delegated official of the administration of urban planning and land use planning of the Walloon Region to intervene in criminal proceedings to pursue there, without being holder of the public action and without having to submit to the rules governing the constitution of civil party, an action which falls within the public action and has as its object a claim of a civil nature?" This case is registered under number 2582 on the Court's roll.

The Registrar, P.-Y. Dutilleux.

COURT OF ARBITRATION

Notice prescribed by article 74 of the special law of 6 January 1989 on the Court of Arbitration By judgment of 3 December 2002 in the matter of the public prosecutor against C. Guinchon and Y. Fourmeau, of which the copy was received at the registry of the Court of Arbitration on 10 December 2002, the Court of Appeal of Bergen posed the following prejudicial question: "Are articles 10 and 11 of the Constitution, as well as article 6 of the European Convention for the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms violated by articles 67, §§ 1 and 2 of the W.W.R.O.S.P., decree of 14 May 1984, and 155, §§ 1 and 2, of the W.W.R.O.S.P., decree of 27 November 1997, which enable an authorised official of the administration of urban planning and spatial planning of the Walloon Region to intervene in criminal proceedings in order to pursue there, without being holder of the criminal action and without having to submit to the rules concerning civil party constitution, a matter which falls under the criminal action and which concerns a claim of a civil nature?"

This case is registered under number 2582 on the Court's roll.

The Registrar, P.-Y. Dutilleux.

COURT OF ARBITRATION

Notice prescribed by article 74 of the Special Law of 6 January 1989 on the Court of Arbitration In its judgment of 3 December 2002 in the matter of the public prosecutor against C. Guinchon and Y. Fourmeau, the copy of which was received on 10 December 2002 at the registry of the Court of Arbitration, the Court of Appeal of Mons posed the following prejudicial question:

"Are articles 10 and 11 of the Constitution and article 6 of the European Convention for the Protection of

Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms violated by articles 67 §§ 1 and 2 of the Walloon Code on Spatial Planning, Urban Planning and Heritage, Decree of 14 May 1984, and 155 §§ 1 and 2 of the Walloon Code on Spatial Planning, Urban Planning and Heritage, Decree of 27 November 1997, which enable an authorised official of the administration for spatial planning and urban planning of the Walloon Region to join in criminal proceedings in order to pursue there, without bringing the public action and without being subject to the rules regarding bringing a civil action, a proceeding which belongs to the sphere of public action and which concerns a claim of a civil nature?" This case was registered under number 2582 in the Court's roll.

The Registrar, P.-Y. Dutilleux.

27 JANUARY 2003. — Decision amending the list of investment firms authorised in Belgium

The Banking and Finance Commission,
Having regard to Article 53 of the Act of 6 April 1995 on the secondary markets, the status of and supervision of investment firms, intermediaries and investment advisers pursuant to which the Banking and Finance Commission draws up the list of investment firms authorised in Belgium;
Having regard to the list of investment firms authorised in Belgium, drawn up on 31 December 2002;
Whereas the Banking and Finance Commission on 30 January 2003 granted to the company ING Investment Management Belgium S.A., abbreviated IIM Belgium, having its registered office at 1000 Brussels, Marnix Avenue 24, an authorisation as a portfolio management company for the following investment services referred to in Article 46, 1°, of the Act of 6 April 1995:
1. a) the reception and transmission on behalf of investors of orders in relation to one or more financial instruments.
3. the management on a discretionary and individual basis of investment portfolios under a mandate given by investors, where such portfolios include one or more financial instruments,
Decides:

The list of investment firms authorised in Belgium, drawn up on 31 December 2002, is amended on 30 January 2003 by the addition in the section "Portfolio management companies" of the company ING Investment Management Belgium S.A., abbreviated IIM Belgium, Marnix Avenue 24, at 1000 Brussels, for investment services 1.a) and 3.
Brussels, 27 January 2003.

E. WYMEERSCH.2. Assessment

Autoliv not being present on the market in question, the market shares of the parties will remain unchanged after the transaction. Furthermore, no player questioned by the Competition Service has made any remarks regarding this concentration. The Council consequently considers that the notified concentration will not have the effect of acquiring or strengthening a dominant position that significantly impedes effective competition on the Belgian market in question or on a substantial part thereof.

FOR THESE REASONS

The Competition Council

Finds that the concentration falls within the scope of the Act of 5 August 1991 on the protection of economic competition and is admissible pursuant to Articles 33 § 1 and 33, § 2, 1.a, of that Act.

Thus decided on 18 April 2002 by the chamber of the Competition Council composed of Mrs Marie-Claude Grégoire, Chamber President, Mr Patrick De Wolf, Mr Pierre Battard and Mr Roger Ramaekers, Members.

FEDERAL PUBLIC SERVICE ECONOMY, SMEs, MIDDLE CLASSES AND ENERGY

[C − 2002/11448]

Competition Council.—Decision No. 2002-C/C-44 of 13 June 2002

Case CONC-C/C-02/0026: VMS-Keytrade.Com S.A.–RealBank S.A.

Having regard to the concentration notification filed on 29 April 2002;

Having regard to the rapporteur's report of 24 May 2002;

Heard at the hearing of 13 June 2002:

—Mr Patrick Marchand, rapporteur;

—Mr Henri Delwaide, lawyer registered with the French-speaking and German-speaking bar association of Brussels, common representative of the notifying parties.

1. The notifying parties

1.1. Acquiring undertaking:

VMS-Keytrade.Com S.A. (hereinafter VMS) is a public limited company under Belgian law having its registered office at 100 boulevard du Souverain, 1170 Brussels, and registered with the Brussels trade register under number 627.795.

Its purpose is to make available to its clients, natural or legal persons, private or institutional, stock exchange companies or banks, both in Belgium and abroad, tools enabling them to manage their own portfolio, in real time or deferred, by electronic means in general and via the Internet in particular.

The company VMS, in practice, concentrates its activities on offering a brokerage service relating to the execution of stock exchange orders. Orders may be transmitted to the company by telephone, fax, mail or directly online via the Internet. On the market for stock transaction execution services (on Euronext Brussels), its market share is less than [business secret]%. However, its status as a stock exchange company did not allow it to be a full-fledged bank.

1.2. Acquired undertaking:

RealBank S.A. (hereinafter RealBank) is a public limited company under Belgian law having its registered office at 1 rue des Colonies, 1000 Brussels, and registered with the Brussels trade register under number 81.690.

Its purpose is banking operations, financial operations, brokerage and commission. It may carry out all enterprises and operations relating directly or indirectly to its purpose or of a nature to promote its achievement. The company may hold shares of associates and participations within the limits provided by the legal statute of banks.

Concretely, this company offers a complete range of banking services, with the exception of credit which remains a marginal activity [business secret].

In the ranking of all Belgian banks established according to the total amount of "customer deposits", Realbank occupied, in 2000, ["between the 50th and 100th position"-business secret]. On the market for stock transaction execution services (on Euronext Brussels), its market share is less than 2%.The public limited company under Belgian law Electrabel having its registered office at 1000 Brussels, Boulevard du Régent 8, is a company active at all stages in the electricity sector, from production to supply to the final customer.

1.2. Seller

The Intercommunale de Distribution d'Energie et de Gaz (hereinafter "Ideg") is an intercommunal association having taken the form of a limited liability cooperative company, established to ensure the distribution of electricity and gas to customers located in the territory of the affiliated municipalities of the province of Namur and two municipalities in Hainaut. Its registered office is established in Namur, avenue Albert Ier 19.

Ideg is a mixed intercommunal association. It is constituted on the basis of a partnership with a private company, namely Electrabel, currently the majority shareholder (to the extent of 62.25%). The municipalities which are associated with it hold 37.75% of its capital.

Regulatory provisions and in particular the provisions of the decree of 5 December 1996 relating to Walloon intercommunal associations, nevertheless provide that the municipalities always have the majority of votes as well as the presidency in the management and control bodies, regardless of the proportion of contributions of the various parties to the constitution of the capital.

1.3. Target undertaking or part of undertaking

The operation concerns Ideg's customer base becoming eligible on the electricity supply market.

2. Description of the operation

2.1. Preamble

The notified concentration concerns the electricity market which is currently in full liberalisation phase. This liberalisation of the electricity market was decided at European level by Directive 96/92 of 19 December 1996 which imposes a limited and progressive opening of the electricity market. This liberalisation is accompanied by regulation which pays particular attention to network access.

Energy in Belgium is a matter whose competences are divided between the federal authority and the Regions. In a very schematic manner, this distribution of powers is established as follows:

—the federal State is competent for matters relating to production, transport and tariffs;

—distribution and supply are regional matters.

The normative framework in this matter comprises:

- the law of 29 April 1999 relating to the organisation of the electricity market (hereinafter "electricity law") published in the Moniteur belge of 11 May 1999;

- the decree of the Flemish Region of 17 July 2000 relating to the organisation of the electricity market (hereinafter "Flemish decree");

- the decree of the Walloon Region of 12 April 2001 relating to the organisation of the regional electricity market (hereinafter "Walloon decree") and the order of the Walloon Government of 21 March 2002 relating to customers who have become eligible and the control of their eligibility;

- the ordinance of 19 July 2001 relating to the organisation of the electricity market in the Brussels-Capital Region (hereinafter "the Ordinance").

The different levels of competence have adopted their own market opening timetables. These differ both from the point of view of eligibility thresholds and dates.

At the end of this liberalisation of the electricity market in Belgium, the distribution of electricity should be divided into two activities to be entrusted to separate companies:

- network management, which concerns the operation, maintenance and development of the distribution network. The network operator must concentrate on its core activity. It may not carry out production activities - other than green electricity - nor sales activities - other than sales to the captive customer base and sales to the so-called protected customer base;

—the sale of electricity which concerns the energy supply of eligible customers.

From 1996, municipalities in partnership with Electrabel had decided to pursue, within mixed intercommunal associations, network distribution management activities and sales to the customer base under municipal monopoly.

Mixed intercommunal associations thus currently ensure the management of the distribution network as well as the supply of electricity. However, article 8, §1 of the Walloon decree relating to the organisation of the regional electricity market applicable to Ideg expressly provides that the distribution network operator may not carry out production activities other than green electricity or electricity sales other than sales necessitated by its activity as network operator. The distribution network operator may not engage in supply activities to eligible customers nor in the supply of other services on the electricity market which are not directly linked to the execution of the tasks referred to in article 11 (i.e. network management). However, at the request of the municipalities, the distribution network operator may supply electricity to captive customers....

7142 MONITEUR BELGE — 12.02.2003 — BELGISCH STAATSBLAD

It follows that the mixed Walloon intercommunal associations, such as Ideg, which wish to be designated as network operator, will no longer be able to simultaneously supply electricity to (newly) eligible customers.

Taking into account the implications of the ongoing liberalisation process, Electrabel and Intermixt Wallonie (public utility establishment bringing together all the public representatives of the mixed intercommunal associations) have thus negotiated the terms of a new agreement capable of maintaining their partnership in a balanced framework.

The principle adopted is that the municipalities and intercommunal associations will become more involved in the management of networks, monopolistic and regulated type activities, while Electrabel will concentrate primarily on the activity of supply to customers. This specialisation of each party is not exclusive: the municipalities via the intercommunal associations will continue to have an interest in the result of the electricity marketing company and Electrabel will retain a shareholding - minority but significant - in the capital of the distribution network operators whose operation it will continue to ensure.

In order to valorise the customer base that these intercommunal associations currently supply with electricity and which they will no longer be able to supply upon their eligibility, the municipalities have agreed with their partner within the mixed intercommunal associations, Electrabel, that a company ECS, established in December 2001 and whose shares are entirely held by Electrabel, will take over this customer base. In consideration for this takeover of control by ECS and this transfer of customer base by Ideg, the municipalities are offered the possibility of increasing their shareholding in the intercommunal association. These municipalities will moreover become minority shareholders (maximum 5% of the capital) in this company ECS and will participate in the profits to the extent of 40% of the result achieved.

These negotiations resulted in the elaboration of various conventions and a Memorandum of Understanding signed on 30 March 2001.

2.2. Operation covered by the notification

The operation which is the subject of this notification consists of the takeover by ECS (established on 12 December 2001 and 100% subsidiary of Electrabel), of Ideg's customer base relating to the supply of electricity to its customers as and when they become eligible and to the extent that they do not decide to conclude a contract with another supplier. An eligible customer means the customer who has the right to conclude a supply contract with the company of their choice and for this purpose has a right of access to the network.

The takeover by ECS of this eligible customer base of Ideg covered by this notification was not the subject of a specific document but must be deduced from a set of documents including the document entitled "Memorandum of Understanding between Intermix – Electrabel signed on 30 March 2001" and the amendments made to the statutes of Ideg adopted on 19 April 2002. The board of directors of Ideg, on 6 June 2001, unanimously decided to have their intercommunal association adhere to the Memorandum of Understanding.

In consideration for this transfer of customer base by Ideg, the municipalities are offered the possibility of increasing their shareholding in the intercommunal association on advantageous terms as well as participating in the profits of ECS to a greater extent than the amount of shares held in it. The participation of the municipalities in the company ECS will in fact be limited to 5% of its capital, but will give the right to a participation in the profits to the extent of 40% of the result.

3. Time limits

The notification was made on 22 May 2002. The time limit referred to in article 33 of the law expires in application of article 33,

#### §§ 1 and 2 of the LPCE, 5 July 2002. 4. Scope of application

The companies ECS and Ideg are undertakings within the meaning of Article 1 of the LPCE. After the concentration, ECS will hold exclusive control of Ideg's activity of supplying electricity to eligible customers (or those who will become so). The transaction therefore constitutes a concentration within the meaning of Article 9, § 1, of the law. On the basis of the figures provided by the parties in the notification, the turnover thresholds referred to in Article 11 of the law are met. 5. Relevant market

5.1. Relevant economic sector

The economic sector concerned by the concentration is that of the production and distribution of electricity (NACE code: 40.1). 5.2. Relevant product market

In the electricity sector, five different types of activities can be distinguished: generation, transmission, distribution, supply and trading. — Generation consists of the production of electricity. — Transmission is the transmission of electricity over high-voltage cables. — Distribution is the transmission of electricity over medium and low-voltage cables. — Supply consists of delivery to the final consumer. — Trading is the purchase and sale of electricity. The first four activities can be considered as belonging to distinct product markets, since they require different assets and resources and because the market and competitive conditions are different for each of them. As for electricity trading, the question of whether it constitutes a separate market from general energy product trading has been left open by the European Commission. Since this market does not constitute a relevant market for the present case, there is no need to rule on this question. Supply of electricity

Following the liberalisation of part of the electricity market initiated by EC Directive 96/92, a further distinction must be made between the supply of electricity to eligible and non-eligible customers (or captive customers, i.e. those who cannot choose their supplier). These belong to two distinct product markets, insofar as competitive conditions are different, and where, most often, they are subject to specific regulations. BELGIAN MONITOR — 12.02.2003 — BELGISCH STAATSBLAD 7143

It is in this context that the present transaction takes place since, pursuant to Article 8, § 1, of the Walloon decree, the distribution network operator is prohibited from any activity of supplying eligible customers. This network operator will probably be the intercommunal authority or a new subsidiary thereof created for this purpose. Electrabel is active directly or indirectly in the markets for production, transmission, distribution, supply of electricity and trading of products related to electricity. Ideg is active in the markets for distribution and supply of electricity. This latter activity, insofar as it concerns customers becoming eligible who have not chosen a supplier, will be carried out by ECS, a 100% subsidiary of Electrabel. Consequently, the relevant product market is that of the supply of electricity to eligible customers. 5.3. Relevant geographic market The geographic market is at most national. Indeed, given the problems related to the limited capacity of interconnections, imports are restricted to a fairly significant extent. 6. Competitive analysis

6.1. Structure of supply: Dominant position of Electrabel

Electrabel occupies a privileged position on the Belgian market since it holds extremely high market shares whatever the market considered. 6.1.1. Supply The market for the supply of electricity to eligible customers in Belgium is estimated by the parties in volume (GWh) at 30,962.4 for the year 2001. Electrabel's market share amounts to more than 90%. No other competitor, whatever its economic and financial power (EDF, RWE, E.ON), individually holds a market share greater than or equal to 5%. 6.1.2. Production Electrabel's dominant position on the market for supply to eligible customers must also be examined taking into account Electrabel's quasi-monopolistic situation on the production market in Belgium. Since 1995, S.A. Electrabel and S.A. SPE have been grouped together within a cooperative company (CPTE) whose purpose is to ensure the coordination of the production and transmission of electrical energy of the two companies. Electrabel is a shareholder up to 91.5%. Of a total production in 2001 estimated at 75,953 GWh by CREG and at [business secrets] GWh by the notifying parties, [business secrets] GWh were produced by Electrabel, i.e. a market share of [more than 90% - the exact percentage falls under business secrecy]. No significant evolution is to be expected, either in the short or medium term on this market. It would even tend to strengthen Electrabel's position. This situation poses all the more problems as electricity imports are at a fairly low level. 6.2. Transmission

The transmission activity concerns the transmission of electricity on the high-voltage network. The cooperative company CPTE, a subsidiary of Electrabel and SPE, owned most of the high-voltage network in Belgium and managed it. On 28 June 2001, the high-voltage network was transferred to a subsidiary company of CPTE, the company ELIA. The latter will be the transmission network operator whose designation, missions and control are regulated by the electricity law and implementing decrees. 6.3. Distribution Electrabel also has a privileged position at distribution level (transmission of electricity from the transmission network to the consumer on a medium and low-voltage network). Indeed, distribution activities fall under the monopoly of municipalities which for the most part have joined together within intercommunal authorities. These intercommunal authorities are for the majority of them qualified as mixed because they include a private partner, in this case in all cases Electrabel which is generally a majority shareholder. Thus, the 16 mixed intercommunal authorities in which Electrabel is a shareholder group together more than 500 municipalities and serve more than 8 million inhabitants in Belgium daily, i.e. more than 82% of the electricity distributed. 6.4. Structure of demand

Currently, the eligibility thresholds in relation to the level of annual consumption are as follows:

- at federal level: more than 20 GWh more than 10 GWh from 31 December 2002; - in the Flemish Region: more than 1 GWh more than 56 Kva from 1 March 2003

- in the Walloon Region: greater than or equal to 20 GWh greater than or equal to 10 GWh by 31 December 2002. - in the Brussels-Capital Region: more than 20 GWh more than 10 GWh from 1 January 2003. 6.5. Barriers to entry

According to the notifying parties, entry to the market is, in terms of costs, relatively easy since it is sufficient to establish a purchase/sale office. In practice, this appears much less obvious. Indeed, the investigation of the file and the hearings have revealed in particular the existence of legal and technical barriers to entry to the Belgian market. 6.5.1. Legal barriers

6.5.1.1. Legal uncertainties

It must be noted that the legislation, which in practice enshrines the right of the eligible customer to obtain supplies from the supplier of his choice, is still incomplete. Certain regulations aimed at ensuring transparent and non-discriminatory access to the network are still missing. This legal vacuum has the effect of de facto favouring the historic operator. 7144 BELGIAN MONITOR — 12.02.2003 — BELGISCH STAATSBLAD

6.5.1.2. Duration of supply contracts/Termination

A transitional system has been put in place in the Walloon Region for customers (becoming) eligible. According to this system, the distribution network operator, being unable to engage in supply activities to eligible customers, designates a supplier for this purpose (in this case Electrabel). The intercommunal authority, two months before the eligibility date, has the obligation to inform the final customer thereof. Once eligible, the customer continues to be supplied by the supplier designated by the operator, failing an agreement made with another supplier. However, he remains free to change supplier subject to one month's notice. The freedom to change supplier and, consequently, to terminate the supply by the designated supplier is total only in the absence of a duly signed contract with the designated supplier. It emerges from the investigation and hearings that a further examination proves necessary on the duration of these contracts. 6.5.2. Technical/physical barriers: interconnection

The importation of electricity from abroad is restricted substantially by the lack of interconnection capacity at the borders. Belgium has interconnection lines with France and the Netherlands. Indeed, in its decision in case IV/M.1803: Electrabel/Epon, the Commission indicates that: "Concerning Belgium, there are also restrictions at the interconnection level: it is noted that theoretical imports from the Netherlands to Belgium represent a negligible quantity."

Not only are interconnection capacities limited, but moreover they are not entirely allocated according to market rules. Indeed, despite an auction system put in place in 2001, it should be emphasised that interconnection capacities are still reserved for Electrabel. The same problem exists at the Franco-Belgian border, all the more so as demand for interconnections is greater in the France-Belgium direction. 6.6. Strengthening of Electrabel's dominant position

The envisaged transaction could be such as to make the market impenetrable for too long a period. Indeed, due to Electrabel's privileged position, the latter already benefits from the aforementioned competitive advantages. It also has access to essential information which competitors do not have or have in a fragmented manner. These include the conditions of access to and use of the network and the list of eligible customers and those who will become so. An illustration of Electrabel's use of this privileged information can be found in its 2001 annual report, where Electrabel states that it deployed in 2001 a very targeted marketing action towards the approximately 2000 customers eligible in Flanders from 1 January 2002. Any barriers must be examined all the more strictly as the market is in full transition phase and the aim is to avoid the establishment of a factual situation damaging to the development of genuine competition, before all the regulatory mechanisms (and in particular regulatory authorities having the full extent of their powers) function effectively. 7. Reaction of companies questioned

All competing companies that responded expressed at the very least clear reservations or even an unfavourable opinion on the present transaction. They confirm the existence of legal and technical barriers to access to the Belgian market and claim to experience significant difficulties in offering competitive contracts. As noted by EDF in its response of 10 June 2002:

"In view of the foregoing, it is therefore possible to consider that the transfer to a single operator of all the eligible clientele of the intercommunal companies could be justified by considerations relating to the necessary reorganisation of the management and distribution of electricity in Belgium under the conditions provided for by the texts. However, it would be different if this transfer were to take place immediately for the benefit of a single operator, without the uncertainties relating in particular to the identification of eligible customers, as well as to the intermediate costs of distribution and transmission of energy, which constitute significant barriers for competitors of the historic operator wishing to access the Belgian market, having been removed. In order to promote fair competition between the historic operator and new players, for the benefit of final consumers, it would therefore be necessary first and foremost to establish and implement clear, transparent and non-discriminatory rules concerning the regimes for access to the transmission and distribution networks for electricity to eligible Belgian customers, as well as the conditions for supplying this electricity in Belgium (regulation, information of eligible customers about suppliers operating on the Belgian market, ease of terminating contracts with the historic operator, in particular)."

The CREG also emphasised the need to conduct a further examination of the contractual relationships that will be entered into between ECS and eligible customers. 8. Conclusion

The elements gathered during the investigation carried out by the Service and during the hearings show that at this stage of the procedure, the notified concentration presents serious doubts as to its compatibility and that it is necessary to initiate the procedure provided for in Article 34 of the L.P.C.E. FOR THESE REASONS

The Competition Council — Notes that the transaction in question falls within the scope of application of the law of 5 August 1991 on the protection of economic competition and that it raises, in accordance with Article 33, § 2, b), serious doubts as to its compatibility; — Decides to initiate the procedure provided for in Article 34 and that it is appropriate to examine in particular:

— The terms and conditions for making information relating to customers who have become eligible available to market players; — The contractual conditions proposed by ECS; — The possible conditions of compatibility of the transaction; — The real capacity of competitors to offer another solution than that envisaged by the transaction. — Invites the corps of rapporteurs to communicate to it a supplementary reasoned report no later than 31 July 2002; Thus ruled on 28 June 2002 by the chamber of the Competition Council composed of Mr. Patrick De Wolf, chamber president, Mrs. Marie-Claude Grégoire and Dominique Smeets and Mr. Pierre Battard, members. BELGIAN MONITOR — 12.02.2003 — BELGISCH STAATSBLAD 7145

FEDERAL PUBLIC SERVICE ECONOMY, SMES, MIDDLE CLASSES AND ENERGY

[C − 2002/11454]

Competition Council.—Decision No. 2002-C/C-50 of 28 June 2002 Case CONC-C/C-02/25: ELECTRABEL CUSTOMER SOLUTIONS S.A./INTERLUX S.C.R.L. In the matter of:

Electrabel Customer Solutions S.A. (hereinafter "ECS"), having its registered office at boulevard du Régent 8, 1000 Brussels and

Intercommunal authority for the distribution of energy for the province of Luxembourg having taken the form of an S.C.R.L. (hereinafter "Interlux"), having its registered office at the town hall of Arlon, rue Paul Reuter 8, 6700 Arlon; Having regard to the law of 5 August 1991 on the protection of economic competition coordinated on 1 July 1999 (hereinafter

LPCE); Having regard to the notification dated 25 April 2002 of a concentration consisting of the taking of exclusive control by ECS, a subsidiary of Electrabel, of Interlux's activity of supplying electricity to the latter's customers as and when they become eligible; Having regard to the case file of the Competition Service; Having regard to the reasoned report of the Corps of Rapporteurs dated 11 June 2002; Having regard to the decision on the confidentiality of the documents in the file of 13 June 2002; Having regard to the request made on the basis of Article 32quater, § 2 LPCE, on 13 June 2002 by S.A. Luminus to be heard in this procedure; Having regard to the decision of 18 June 2002 granting this request; Having regard to the request dated 20 June 2002 and transmitted to the Competition Council on 21 June 2002 by which the Commission for the Regulation of Electricity and Gas (abbreviated CREG) requests on the basis of Article 32quater, § 2 LPCE and Article 23 of the law of 29 April 1999 on the organisation of the electricity market, to be heard in this procedure; Having regard to the decision of 24 June 2002 granting this request; Having heard at the hearing of 26 June 2002, S.A. Luminus and the Commission for the Regulation of Electricity and Gas; Having heard at the hearing of 27 June 2002 Mr. rapporteur P. Marchand and the notifying parties represented by Mr. de Garcia and Mr. Baeten and assisted by Me Vandencasteele, Me Brinckman and Me Vronincks, lawyers in Brussels; 1. The notifying parties

1.1. Acquirer

The company Electrabel Customer Solutions (hereinafter "ECS") is a public limited company under Belgian law incorporated by deed of 12 December 2001 published in the annexes to the Moniteur belge of 28 December 2001, having its registered office at 1000 Brussels, boulevard du Régent 8, and having as its object the supply of electricity and gas as well as the supply of related products and services. This company is in fact a 100% subsidiary of S.A. Electrabel. The public limited company under Belgian law Electrabel having its registered office at 1000 Brussels, boulevard du Régent 8, is an undertaking active at all stages in the electricity sector, from production to supply to the final customer. 1.2. Seller

The Intercommunal authority for the distribution of energy for the province of Luxembourg (hereinafter "Interlux") is an intercommunal association having taken the form of a cooperative company with limited liability, established to ensure the distribution of energy in the province of Luxembourg. Its main activity is the distribution of electricity and gas to customers located on the territory of the affiliated municipalities. Its registered office is established at the town hall of Arlon, rue Paul Reuter 8. Interlux is a mixed intercommunal authority. It is based on a partnership with a private company, in this case Electrabel, currently majority shareholder (up to 69%). The municipalities associated with it hold 31% of the capital. Regulatory provisions and in particular the provisions of the decree of 5 December 1996 on Walloon intercommunal authorities, however provide that the municipalities always have the majority of votes as well as the chairmanship in the management and control bodies, regardless of the proportion of the contributions of the various parties to the formation of the capital. 1.3. Target undertaking or part of undertaking

The transaction concerns Interlux's clientele becoming eligible on the electricity supply market. 2. Description of the transaction

2.1. Preamble

The notified concentration concerns the electricity market which is currently in full liberalisation phase. This liberalisation of the electricity market was decided at European level by Directive 96/92 of 19 December 1996 which requires a limited and progressive opening of the electricity market. This liberalisation is accompanied by regulation which pays particular attention to network access. Energy in Belgium is a matter whose powers are divided between the federal authority and the Regions. Very schematically, this distribution of powers is established as follows: — the federal State is competent for matters relating to production, transmission and tariffs; — distribution and supply are regional matters. 7146 BELGIAN MONITOR — 12.02.2003 — BELGISCH STAATSBLAD

The normative framework in this matter includes:

— the law of 29 April 1999 on the organisation of the electricity market (hereinafter "electricity law") published in the Moniteur belge of 11 May 1999; — the decree of the Flemish Region of 17 July 2000 on the organisation of the electricity market (hereinafter "Flemish decree"); — the decree of the Walloon Region of 12 April 2001 on the organisation of the regional electricity market (hereinafter "Walloon decree") and the decree of the Walloon Government of 21 March 2002 on customers becoming eligible and the control of their eligibility; — the ordinance of 19 July 2001 on the organisation of the electricity market in the Brussels-Capital Region (hereinafter "the ordinance"). The different levels of competence have adopted their own market opening timetables. These differ both in terms of eligibility thresholds and dates. At the end of this liberalisation of the electricity market in Belgium, electricity distribution should be split into two activities to be entrusted to separate companies:

- network management, which covers the operation, maintenance and development of the distribution network. The network operator must concentrate on its main activity. It may not carry out production activities - other than green electricity - or sales activities - other than sales to captive customers and sales to so-called protected customers. — the sale of electricity which concerns the supply of energy to eligible customers. Now, since 1996, municipalities in partnership with Electrabel had decided to continue, within mixed intercommunal authorities, network distribution management and sales activities to customers under municipal monopoly. Mixed intercommunal authorities thus currently ensure the management of the distribution network as well as the supply of electricity. However, Article 8, § 1, of the Walloon decree on the organisation of the regional electricity market applicable to Interlux expressly provides that the distribution network operator may not carry out production activities other than green electricity or electricity sales other than sales required for its activity as network operator. The distribution network operator may not engage in supply activities to eligible customers nor in the supply of other services on the electricity market which are not directly linked to the performance of the tasks referred to in Article 11 (i.e. network management). However, at the request of the municipalities, the distribution network operator may supply electricity to captive customers... It follows that Walloon mixed intercommunal authorities, such as Interlux, which wish to be designated as network operator, will no longer be able to supply electricity to (formerly) eligible customers at the same time. Given the implications of the ongoing liberalisation process, Electrabel and Intermixt Wallonie (public utility establishment bringing together all public representatives of mixed intercommunal authorities) have thus negotiated the terms of a new agreement capable of maintaining their partnership in a balanced framework. The principle adopted is that the municipalities and intercommunal authorities will be more involved in network management, monopolistic and regulated activities, while Electrabel will concentrate mainly on the activity of supplying customers. This specialisation of each party is not exclusive: the municipalities via the intercommunal authorities will continue to be interested in the result of the electricity marketing company and Electrabel will retain a shareholding — minority but significant — in the capital of the distribution network operators whose operation it will continue to ensure. In order to value the clientele that these intercommunal authorities currently supply with electricity and that they will no longer be able to supply from the time of their eligibility, the municipalities have agreed with their partner within the mixed intercommunal authorities, Electrabel, that a company ECS, incorporated in December 2001 and whose shares are entirely held by Electrabel, will take over this clientele. In return for this taking of control by ECS and this transfer of clientele by Interlux, the municipalities are offered the possibility of increasing their shareholding in the intercommunal authority. These municipalities will also become minority shareholders (maximum 5% of the capital) in this company ECS and will participate in the profits up to 40% of the result achieved. These negotiations resulted in the drafting of various agreements and a Memorandum of Understanding signed on 30 March 2001. 2.2. Transaction covered by the notification The transaction which is the subject of this notification consists of the takeover by ECS (incorporated on 12 December 2001 and a 100% subsidiary of Electrabel), of Interlux's clientele for the supply of electricity to the latter's customers as and when they become eligible and insofar as they do not decide to conclude a contract with another supplier. An eligible customer is understood to mean the customer who has the right to conclude a supply contract with the undertaking of his choice and for this purpose has a right of access to the network. The takeover by ECS of this eligible clientele of Interlux covered by this notification has not been the subject of a specific document but must be deduced from a set of documents including the document entitled "Memorandum of understanding between Intermixt-Electrabel" signed on 30 March 2001 and the amendments to the articles of association of Interlux adopted on 25 March 2002. 3. Time limits

The notification made on 25 April 2002 was incomplete on one important point within the meaning of Article 5, § 2, of the Royal Decree of 23 March 1993 on the notification of concentrations of undertakings: it did not contain in particular the agreement constituting the notified concentration, nor the minutes of the General Meeting of 25 March 2002 amending the articles of association of Interlux. The missing information was provided by the parties to the rapporteur on 13 May 2002. The time limit referred to in Article 33 of the law therefore starts to run on 14 May 2002 and expires in application of Article 33, §§ 1 and 2 of the LPCE, on 28 June 2002.
