4. Scope of application

The companies ECS and Interlux are undertakings within the meaning of Article 1 of the LPCE. After the concentration, ECS will hold exclusive control of Interlux's activity of supplying electricity to eligible customers (or those who will become so). The transaction therefore constitutes a concentration within the meaning of Article 9, § 1, of the law. On the basis of the figures provided by the parties in the notification, the turnover thresholds referred to in Article 11 of the law are met. 5. Relevant market

Barriers to entry

According to the notifying parties, entry to the market is, in terms of costs, relatively easy since it is sufficient to establish a purchase/sale office. In practice, this appears much less obvious. Indeed, the investigation of the file and the hearings have revealed in particular the existence of legal and technical barriers to entry to the Belgian market. 6.5.1. Legal barriers

6.5.1.1. Legal uncertainties It must be noted that the legislation, which in practice enshrines the right of the eligible customer to obtain supplies from the supplier of his choice, is still incomplete.Certain regulations are still missing aimed at ensuring transparent and non-discriminatory access to the network. This legal void has the effect of de facto favoring the historic operator.

6.5.1.2. Duration of supply contracts/Termination

A transitional system has been put in place in the Walloon Region for (becoming) eligible customers. According to this system, the distribution network operator, being unable to engage in supply activities to eligible customers, designates a supplier for that purpose (in this case Electrabel). The intermunicipal company has the obligation to inform the final customer two months before the eligibility date. Once eligible, the customer continues to be supplied by the supplier designated by the operator, in the absence of an agreement entered into with another supplier. However, the customer remains free to change supplier subject to one month's notice.

An illustration of Electrabel's use of this privileged information is found in its 2001 annual report, where Electrabel states that it deployed in 2001 a very targeted marketing action towards the approximately 2000 customers eligible in Flanders from 1 January 2002.

7. Reaction of the companies questioned

All competing companies that responded expressed at the very least clear reservations or even an unfavorable opinion on the present operation.

They confirm the existence of legal and technical barriers to access to the Belgian market and claim to experience significant difficulties in offering competitive contracts.

As EDF notes in its response of 10 June 2002:

"In view of the foregoing, it is therefore possible to consider that the transfer to a single operator of all the eligible customers of the intermunicipal companies could be justified by considerations relating to the necessary reorganization of the management and distribution of electricity in Belgium under conditions provided for in the regulations.

However, it would be different if this transfer were to take place immediately for the benefit of a single operator, without the uncertainties having been removed relating in particular to the identification of eligible customers, as well as to the intermediate costs of distribution and transport of energy, which constitute significant barriers for competitors of the historic operator wishing to access the Belgian market.

In order to promote fair competition between the historic operator and new players, for the benefit of final consumers, it would therefore be necessary first and as a priority to establish and implement clear, transparent and non-discriminatory rules concerning the regimes of access to electricity transmission and distribution networks for eligible Belgian customers, as well as the conditions for supply of this electricity in Belgium (regulation, information of eligible customers on suppliers operating on the Belgian market, ease of termination of contracts with the historic operator, in particular)."

The CREG has also emphasized the need to carry out a further examination of the contractual relationships that will be established between ECS and eligible customers.

8. Conclusion

The elements gathered during the investigation carried out by the Service and during the hearings reveal that at this stage of the procedure, the notified concentration raises serious doubts as to its admissibility and that it is necessary to initiate the procedure provided for in Article 34 of the L.P.C.E.

FOR THESE REASONS

The Competition Council

— Notes that the operation in question falls within the scope of the law of 5 August 1991 on the protection of economic competition and that it raises, in accordance with Article 33, § 2, b), serious doubts as to its admissibility;

— Decides to initiate the procedure provided for in Article 34 and that it is appropriate to examine in particular:

— The procedures for making available information relating to customers who have become eligible to market players;

— The contractual conditions proposed by ECS;

— The possible conditions of admissibility of the operation;

— The actual capacity of competitors to offer another solution than that envisaged by the operation.

— Invites the body of rapporteurs to submit to it a supplementary reasoned report no later than 31 July 2002;

Thus ruled on 28 June 2002 by the chamber of the Competition Council composed of Mr. Patrick De Wolf, chamber president, Mrs. Marie-Claude Grégoire and Dominique Smeets and Mr. Pierre Battard, members.

FEDERAL PUBLIC SERVICE ECONOMY, SMEs, MIDDLE CLASSES AND ENERGY

[C − 2002/11451]

Competition Council.—Decision No. 2002-E/A-47 of 27 June 2002

In the matter of:

— the société anonyme under French law Club Méditerranée (hereinafter Club Med) whose registered office is established at 11, rue de Cambrai, 75019 Paris.

Having regard to the notification of the standard contract establishing a new distribution network for Club Med products characterized by territorial exclusivity, received on 28 February 2002 and registered with the Competition Council under number CONC-E/A-01/0014 by which the company Club Med requests:

— as a principal claim, the benefit of the granting of a negative clearance provided for in Article 6 of the law of 5 August 1991 on the protection of economic competition (hereinafter referred to as L.P.C.E.);

— as a subsidiary claim, the filing of the dossier on the basis of Article 32 of that law;

— as a very subsidiary claim, the granting of an individual exemption under Article 2, § 3, of that law;

Having regard to the reasoned report of the body of rapporteurs of 4 February 2002, notified to the company Club Med on the same day;

Having heard at the hearing of 25 March 2002, the body of rapporteurs and the notifying party, the company Club Med, represented by Mr. Jeff Van Bever, General Director, and Mrs. Maria Pia Nardone, assisted by their counsel, Mr. Peter Wytinck, lawyer in Brussels;

Whereas the composition of the chamber had to be modified for reasons of force majeure;

Having heard at the hearing of 27 June 2002, the body of rapporteurs and the notifying party, the company Club Med, represented by Mr. Jeff Van Bever, General Director, and Mrs. Maria Pia Nardone, assisted by their counsel, Mr. Peter Wytinck, lawyer in Brussels;

Whereas the company Club Med operates as a generalist tour operator, which consists essentially of the design and marketing, in nearly 50 countries, of a category of holidays based on the "all-inclusive system", including transport, accommodation, catering and entertainment.

That according to the notifying party, the distribution network put in place is based on a standard exclusive agent contract. This contract will have the nature of an agency contract insofar as the final customers will be contractually bound to Club Med and not to the agencies.

7150 MONITEUR BELGE — 12.02.2003 — BELGISCH STAATSBLAD

That in order to ensure coverage of the entire Belgian territory, in each arrondissement or municipality (depending on population density or economic potential) Club Med products will be marketed by one or more agencies, chosen according to the commercial interests of Club Med and their capacity to market these products. The selected agencies will benefit from territorial exclusivity.

That the contract is concluded for a period of three years, that is to say from 1 January 2002 to 31 December 2004.

Admissibility of the request

Whereas the request for negative clearance and exemption introduced by the company Club Med must be declared admissible given that it is a legal person pursuing a lasting economic objective (and is therefore an undertaking within the meaning of Article 1 of the law) and that it justifies a sufficient interest by acting with a view to obtaining guarantees on the validity of its standard distribution contract with regard to the law.

Reasons invoked by the notifying party to justify the choice of a closed distribution network

Whereas the notifying party states that its commercial policy is to ensure not only a constant improvement in the quality of its final products but also to offer above all before any sale, quality advice service to customers.

That to do this, it considers that the competence of its staff is essential, and that this requires ongoing training. This requires significant investments in terms of financial and personal resources both on its part and on that of its local agencies. It maintains that this training is only possible if the distribution network is limited and claims that otherwise, the additional investments could only be passed on to the commitments it requests from approved agencies, with the consequence of a price increase demanded from customers.

That moreover, it also maintains that open distribution would jeopardize the willingness of agencies to invest in the sale of Club Med products because profitability would be called into question.

That according to the company Club Med, the distribution network as envisaged will enable agencies to respond in a global, adequate and rapid manner to the demand for its products and in a profitable manner for them. It is thus justified according to it, to opt for a closed distribution network for its products.

The market

The product or service market concerned

Whereas the company Club Med proposes to retain as the relevant market, the market for package holidays abroad departing from Belgium.

Whereas the body of rapporteurs considers that Club Med products do indeed fall within the definition of package tours and that it could be possible in this case to envisage a segmentation of the package tour market according to the holiday formula (holidays in club village formula or others...), especially as Club Méditerranée emphasizes the specific characteristics of Club Med products to justify the closed nature of its distribution network.

That however, the body of rapporteurs notes that the reactions of the competitors of the company Club Méditerranée on the segmentation to be retained do not go in this direction. Tour operators such as Ticket BBL Travel, VIP Travel, Sunsnacks (Thomas Cook), VTB Reizen agree with Club Méditerranée's presentation.

That only Nouvelles Frontières segments its activity according to three segments that correspond to the segmentation carried out in its brochure.

That in its decision Preussag/Nouvelles Frontières of 14 November 2000 (No. COMP/M.2186), the Commission considered Club Med as a competitor of Bosphorus, Nouvelles Frontières etc... on the package tour market in Belgium, with a market share of 3-4%.

That in conclusion, the Rapporteur proposes to retain as the relevant market, like the notifying party, the market for package holidays abroad departing from Belgium.

Whereas the notifying company also refers to the decision of the European Commission of 22 September 1999 rendered in case IV/M.1524 Airtours/First Choice (OJ L-93 of 13 April 2000) to consider that the definition of the relevant market to be retained as far as it is concerned taken up in the judgment of the Brussels Court of Appeal of 22 April 1999 was not well-founded.

Whereas since then the Court of First Instance of the European Communities has ruled on this decision by its judgment of 6 June 2002 (T342/99).

Whereas the product market in question includes all products and/or services that the consumer considers interchangeable or substitutable by reason of their characteristics, their price and the use for which they are intended (Commission notice, OJ 1997 C/372, p. 51).

Whereas the Competition Council has also already ruled in concentration cases concerning this sector (decision No. 2001-C/C-32 of 18 June 2001, Ticket BBL Travel/Reizen Theo Huybrechts/Kroon Reizen/L'Epervier, decision No. 2001-C/C-48 of 19 September 2001, C&N Touristic Belgium N.V./City Bird Holding S.A./City Bird S.A. and decision No. 2002-C/C-22 of 20 February 2002, American Express International Inc/BBL Travel American Express S.A.).

That the aforementioned decisions all concern concentrations.

That it is appropriate to note that the present procedure concerns a matter covered by Article 2 L.P.C.E.

Whereas the Competition Council considers, in this case and on the basis of the elements in its possession, that it cannot necessarily retain the market for package holidays abroad departing from Belgium, as the relevant market in the present case.

That indeed, as the company Club Med itself points out, to justify the closed nature of its distribution network, the product it offers is a specific product based on "all-inclusive" and including in the package price transport, accommodation, catering and entertainment.

That moreover, the Brussels Court of Appeal, by its judgment of 22 September 1999, noted several particular and characteristic elements that would demonstrate that it is a specific market, and in particular the particular service offered, the formula of holiday villages offering particular entertainment.

Whereas therefore, it is appropriate to invite the body of rapporteurs to define the relevant market taking into account the degree of substitutability of the services offered, both by S.A. Club Med and by its competitors, and furthermore, to assess the market share of the notifying party, on this market thus defined.

Analysis of the contract

Whereas in the notification, the company Club Med. transmitted a standard contract model.

Whereas this standard contract has been used since 1 January 2002;

MONITEUR BELGE — 12.02.2003 — BELGISCH STAATSBLAD 7151

That it is appropriate to examine the contracts already entered into in order to be better able to rule on the request formulated by the notifying company;

That the scope that is or will be given to certain clauses of this contract, also proves important;

That at the hearing of this 27 June 2002, a copy of a contract has already been filed;

That it is important to obtain communication of a copy of all contracts that have entered into force since 1 January 2002 and covered by the present notification, in particular because of the clauses left to the parties' discretion;

FOR THESE REASONS,

the Competition Council

— decides that it is appropriate to invite the body of rapporteurs to define the relevant market taking into account the degree of substitutability of the services offered, both by S.A. Club Med and by its competitors, and furthermore, to assess the market share of the notifying party, on this market thus defined.

— Orders the notifying company to communicate to the body of rapporteurs a copy of all exclusive agent contracts (and amendments) in force since 1 January 2002.

Thus ruled on 27 June 2002 by the chamber of the Competition Council composed of Mr. Patrick De Wolf, chamber president, Mrs. Béatrice Ponet and Dominique Smeets, Mr. Eric Balate, members.

FEDERAL PUBLIC SERVICE ECONOMY, SMEs, MIDDLE CLASSES AND ENERGY

[C − 2002/11432]

Competition Council.—Decision No. 2002-P/K-29 of 9 April 2002

Case CONC-I/O-97/0016

In the matter of:

Stora Enso Langerbrugge, (hereinafter referred to as the complainant) société anonyme under Belgian law having its registered office at Wondelgemkaai 200, 9000 Ghent, is a subsidiary of the Stora group of which Stora Corbehem s.a., a company under French law whose registered office is located Rue de Brebières at Corbehem (France), is also part.

Against

Fruytier Scierie, société anonyme under Belgian law having its registered office at 3, rue Saint-Isidore, 6900 Marche-en-Famenne, active in forestry and the sawing of softwood.

Having regard to the law on the protection of economic competition, coordinated on 1 July 1999;

Having regard to the complaint filed on 25 August 1997 by the complainant with the Competition Service and registered on the same day under number CONC-I/O-97/0016;

Having regard to the report of the Body of rapporteurs of 24 January 2002;

Having heard at the hearing of 9 April 2002, Mrs. Anne Bouillet, for the body of rapporteurs, and the complainant, represented by its counsel Miguel Troncoso-Ferrer and Hugo Keulers.

Whereas the present procedure was introduced on 25 August 1997 in application of the law of 5 August 1991 on the protection of economic competition (hereinafter L.P.C.E.), which was amended by two laws of 26 April 1999 then coordinated by royal decree on 1 July 1999;

Whereas Article 47 of the law of 26 April 1999 amending the law of 5 August 1991 on the protection of economic competition states that the provisions of the new law "do not apply to proceedings initiated before the Competition Council or the Brussels Court of Appeal at the time of entry into force of this law", that is 1 October 1999;

Whereas the text concerning prescription has not been modified;

Whereas the Competition Council considers that a procedure is pending before it from the moment it has been seized by the report of the Body of rapporteurs;

Whereas in this case, the report of the Body of rapporteurs having been transmitted to the Competition Council on 24 January 2002, the amended law is applicable;

Whereas the complaint concerned a contract signed between the parties on 14 September 1995, a contract that the complainant considered restrictive of competition.

Whereas the relevant product market is that of white TMP chips (thermo mechanical pulp) from the sawing of spruce;

That these chips form the raw material necessary for the production of a certain type of quality paper manufactured by Stora Langerbrugge, SC paper (super-calendered mechanical printing paper) used for printing magazines;

That there is no product truly substitutable for white TMP chips for the production of SC paper.

Whereas the relevant geographic market must be delimited to the entire Belgian territory, Luxembourg, part of western Germany and eastern France, that is within a radius of 300 km around the sawmill, due to the high transport cost of white TMP chips.

Whereas on 17 September 1997, the complainant's representatives informed the Service by letter of the withdrawal of the complaint, the contract in question no longer being in force;

That consequently, the file should be closed, the complaint having become moot.

For these reasons,

The Competition Council

— Receives the complaint;

— Notes that the case registered under number CONC-I/O-97/0016 has become moot;

— Consequently closes the file;

Thus decided on 9 April 2002 by the chamber of the Competition Council composed of Mr. Jacques Schaar, chamber president, Mrs. Marie-Claude Grégoire, Mrs. Dominique Smeets, and Mr. David Szafran, members.

7152 MONITEUR BELGE — 12.02.2003 — BELGISCH STAATSBLAD

Competition Council.—Decision No. 2002-P/K-30 of 9 April 2002

Case CONC-PRA-95/0014

In the matter of:

Computer Media s.a., company under Belgian law having its registered office at 97, rue Reper Vreven, 1020 Brussels, hereinafter referred to as the complainant.

Against

AT&T Belgium s.a., company under Belgian law having its registered office at 50, rue de la Fusée, 1130 Brussels.

Having regard to the law on the protection of economic competition, coordinated on 1 July 1999;

Having regard to the complaint filed on 6 July 1995 by the complainant with the Competition Service and registered on 11 July 1995 under number CONC-PRA-95/0014;

Having regard to the report of the Body of rapporteurs of 30 January 2002;

Having heard at the hearing of 9 April 2002, Mrs. Anne Bouillet, for the body of rapporteurs, at which hearing the complainant, although duly summoned, did not appear nor anyone on its behalf.

Whereas the present procedure was introduced on 6 July 1995 in application of the law of 5 August 1991 on the protection of economic competition (hereinafter L.P.C.E.), which was amended by two laws of 26 April 1999 then coordinated by royal decree on 1 July 1999; that it should be noted that, since the introduction of the procedure, the complainant has no longer manifested itself;

Whereas Article 47 of the law of 26 April 1999 amending the law of 5 August 1991 on the protection of economic competition states that the provisions of the new law "do not apply to proceedings initiated before the Competition Council or the Brussels Court of Appeal at the time of entry into force of this law", that is 1 October 1999;

Whereas the text concerning prescription has not been modified;

Whereas the Competition Council considers that a procedure is pending before it from the moment it has been seized by the report of the Body of rapporteurs;

Whereas in this case, the report of the Body of rapporteurs having been transmitted to the Competition Council on 30 January 2002, the amended law is applicable;

Whereas Article 48, § 1, of the L.P.C.E. provides that "The investigation referred to in Article 23 may only concern facts not going back more than five years. This period is calculated from the date of the decision of the Competition Service to proceed with an investigation ex officio or from the date of referral of the Service in accordance with Article 23,

#### § 1

er."; that Article 48, § 2, of the L.P.C.E. provides that "The limitation period with regard to the investigation and decision procedure is five years from the date referred to in § 1. The limitation period shall only be interrupted by acts of investigation or decision made within the period determined in the preceding paragraph; such acts give rise to a new period of equal duration."

Whereas the Competition Council notes that no act of investigation has been carried out in this file for more than five years, that is, since the Competition Service, by fax dated 14 September 1995, invited the complainant to an information meeting to be held on 5 October 1995, a meeting of which no trace appears in the file; Whereas the limitation period provided for by Article 48, § 2, of the L.P.C.E. has therefore been reached; that the file should consequently be closed. For these reasons, The Competition Council

—Receives the complaint

—Notes that the case registered under number CONC-PRA-95/0014 is time-barred; —Closes the file accordingly; Thus decided on 9 April 2002 by the chamber of the Competition Council composed of Mrs Marie-Claude Grégoire, chamber president, Mrs Dominique Smeets, Mr Jacques Schaar, and Mr David Szafran, members. FEDERAL PUBLIC SERVICE ECONOMY, SMEs, MIDDLE CLASSES AND ENERGY

[C − 2002/11447]

Competition Council.—Decision No. 2002-V/M-43 of 13 June 2002 Case CONC-V/M-02/0008: INTERDAMO S.A./ITM Belgium S.A. and Société centrale d'Approvisionnement en Produits régionaux S.A. I. The parties involved

1. the applicant for interim measures

The applicant for interim measures is the S.A. under Belgian law INTERDAMO whose registered office is established at 4633 Melen, rue du Parc Hauzeur 70, registered in the commercial register of Verviers under number 68.141, which operates a supermarket-type business under the Intermarché sign. 2. The incriminated parties

2.1. S.A. ITM Belgium

The S.A. under Belgian law ITM Belgium, (hereinafter referred to as S.A. ITM or ITM) is registered in the commercial register of Nivelles under number 90.049 and has its registered office established at 1348 Louvain-la-Neuve, Traverse d'Esope 6. It represents the limited company under French law, ITM Entreprises (hereinafter S.A. ITME) of which it is a subsidiary and has as its object the establishment and promotion of a franchise network for distribution in supermarkets under the Intermarché sign or related signs. MONITEUR BELGE — 12.02.2003 — BELGISCH STAATSBLAD 7153

2.2. S.A. Société centrale d'Approvisionnement en Produits régionaux The S.A. under Belgian law Société centrale d'Approvisionnement en Produits régionaux (hereinafter referred to as S.A. SCA) is registered in the commercial register of Namur under number 72.540 and has its registered office established at 5000 Beez, rue de Namur 45. This company, a subsidiary of ITM, is responsible for purchasing goods and supplying the network's franchisees ("Mousquetaires"). II. Background to the interim measures procedure S.A. Interdamo, by a complaint dated 14 January 2002 and filed on 18 January 2002, denounced a violation of Article 2 of the law of 5 August 1991 on the protection of economic competition against S.A. ITM Belgium and Société centrale d'Approvisionnement en produits régionaux and requested that the president of the Competition Council take, in accordance with Article 35 of that law, interim measures aimed at "suspending the rules in dispute". By letter dated 13 February 2002, the complaining party and applicant for interim measures informed that it wished to file an amplified complaint. The latter was filed on 20 February 2002. The Body of Rapporteurs transmitted its reasoned report to the secretariat of the Competition Council on 28 February 2002. The hearing before the Competition Council was set for 21 March 2002. By letter dated 18 March 2002, the applicant party for interim measures, through one of its counsel, informed the Competition Council that the president of the Commercial Court of Liège had appointed ex officio an interim administrator for its client, having as first mission to summon it to bankruptcy and that in these conditions, there was reason to fear that this action had become without interest. By letter dated 20 March 2002, this lawyer informed the Competition Council that two interim administrators had been appointed on 19 March 2002 by the president of the Commercial Court of Liège and requested a postponement sine die of the interim measures hearing of 21 March 2002, the time to allow the interim administrators to become acquainted with the dispute and form an opinion on it. By fax dated 20 March 2002, the Secretary of the Competition Council indicated in accordance with the instructions that had been given to him, that the hearing of 21 March was maintained and that if necessary, it was always possible to consider a continuation of the case in order to allow the interim administrators to express themselves or to designate a representative for that purpose. On 21 March 2002, the examination of the case was initiated in the presence of the Body of Rapporteurs represented by

Mr P. Marchand, rapporteur and the Counsel of the incriminated parties. The applicant party being neither present nor represented, the case was continued to 18 April 2002 in order to allow the appointed interim administrators to intervene. By letter dated 11 April 2002, one of the interim administrators of S.A Interdamo informed that he would not appear at the hearing of 18 April, the lawyer initially representing the company Interdamo being responsible for defending the position of this company. By fax dated 18 April 2002, the latter informed that S.A. Interdamo would not appear at the hearing of the same day and that in view of its situation and the fact that a summons to bankruptcy had been filed by the interim administrators, it no longer intended to pursue the interim measures applications which were without object. III. Facts of the case

The applicant for interim measures, S.A Interdamo operates a supermarket-type business mainly focused on food products, under the Intermarché sign. This company indeed signed on 1 July 1996 a sign agreement (franchise) with S.A. ITM Belgium, exclusive holder on Belgian territory of the exploitation rights to the trademark relating to the Intermarché sign or related signs. The managing director of S.A. Interdamo, Dominique Langlet and his wife, Anne-Marie Langlet-Dejasse were co-signatories of this agreement. The Langlet-Dejasse spouses also signed with the S.A. under French law ITM Entreprises whose registered office is at 75015 Paris, rue Auguste Chabrières 24, an agreement of adhesion to the "Réseau des Mousquetaires" (on a date not specified in this agreement) through S.A. under Belgian law ITM Belgium mandated for that purpose. These contracts were concluded for a period of ten years. From 1997, the parties mutually accused each other of not respecting the rules and principles contained in these agreements. It thus appears in particular from the documents transmitted by the applicant for interim measures, that from 26 March 1997, S.A. Interdamo had a bailiff record the inventory of products ordered but not delivered by the company responsible for supplying this point of sale. The managing director of S.A. Interdamo maintains that this situation had already been continuing for approximately two months. From August 2000, many reproaches were formulated on both sides: —S.A. ITM thus reproached the Langlet spouses and S.A. Interdamo in particular for a lack of spirit of solidarity and autonomous and too costly advertising campaigns; —S.A. Interdamo and the Langlet spouses for their part reproached S.A. SCA and S.A. ITM for a lack of punctuality in deliveries as well as deficiencies in the information they were required to transmit to them. In June 2001, Mrs A.-M. Dejasse appeared officially as managing director of S.A. Interdamo in place of her husband. In September 2001, Mr Langlet, majority shareholder of S.A. Interdamo informed S.A. ITM of his decision to reorient his professional career and to transfer his shares to his wife for a price of 36 million (BEF). In November 2001, S.A. ITM learned that Mr Langlet had allegedly been employed by a competing company, namely the company Carrefour. Mrs Dejasse, confirmed this information and was prohibited on 28 November 2001, from participating in a coordination meeting at the headquarters of S.A. ITM. The interested party had this fact recorded by the bailiff Martine Cuisenaire. S.A. ITM, moreover, by letter dated 5 December 2001, informed Mr Langlet that it was exercising the purchase option provided for in the adhesion contract while contesting the transfer price. According to the applicant party for interim measures, S.A. SCA allegedly modified from 29 November 2001, the order of the rounds of its trucks from the Villers-le-Bouillet base in order to delay the delivery time of the point of sale that S.A. Interdamo operates in Battice. This had the effect, while it was usually delivered before 5:30, of only being supplied with perishable goods from that date shortly before 7 o'clock and sometimes even later (8:20 on 15 December 2001, 7:10 on 27 December 2001, 7:45 on 29 January 2002). Other failures relating to the communication of information necessary for operation or to the supply of advertising furniture were also allegedly committed according to S.A. Interdamo, at the end of 2001. 7154 MONITEUR BELGE — 12.02.2003 — BELGISCH STAATSBLAD

By summons in interim proceedings of 18 December 2001, S.A. Interdamo requested the president of the commercial court of Verviers to order the suspension of the execution of the sign agreement on grounds of deliberate failures by S.A. ITM reflecting its will to no longer collaborate loyally and in good faith with it. An order was rendered in the context of this action on 18 January 2002 and declared the claims formulated in principal and in the alternative receivable but unfounded. Appeal was filed against this decision by S.A. Interdamo. On 18 January 2002, S.A. Interdamo filed a complaint with the Competition Council and requested that interim measures be taken in accordance with Article 35 of the law of 5 August 1991 on the protection of economic competition. In view of the dispute between the parties concerning the ownership of the shares of S.A. Interdamo formerly held by Mr Langlet and the risks that they might be resold to a third party, S.A. ITM, on unilateral application, obtained on 7 February 2002 an order from the president of the Commercial Court of Liège, for the sequestration of these shares. By judgment of 15 February 2002, the Court of Appeal of Liège confirmed the interim order of 18 January 2002. This judgment recognized the specificities of the Intermarché network and considered that the passage of Dominique Langlet to an important competitor (Carrefour), when he was the essential element of S.A. Interdamo, appeared as a direct violation of the adhesion contract and consequently a wrongful breach of the obligations contained in the sign agreement. The wish of S.A. ITM to take over the shares of S.A. Interdamo of which Dominique Langlet was the holder was in line with what this contract provides. Moreover, this judgment refuted the various grievances advanced by S.A. Interdamo by noting that they did not rest on a culpable failure, an abuse of right or bad will in the case of S.A. ITM. On 19 February 2002, Interdamo filed an amplified complaint with the Competition Council. By order of 19 March 2002, the president of the Commercial Court of Liège considered that the conditions of bankruptcy appeared to be met in the case of S.A. Interdamo and that there was urgency and absolute necessity to divest its organs of their management powers. In accordance with Article 8 of the law on bankruptcies of 8 August 1997, two interim administrators were thus appointed with a view in particular to summoning this company to bankruptcy. By fax dated 18 April 2002, one of the counsel of S.A. Interdamo informed the Competition Council that it no longer intended to pursue the interim measures applications which were without object. IV. Grievances invoked by S.A. Interdamo S.A Interdamo considers that the sign agreements and adhesion agreements are contrary to Article 2 of the law of 5 August 1991 on the protection of economic competition given that they constitute an agreement between the different contracting parties (enterprises), which creates a network controlled by S.A. ITM for its sole and unique profit, to the detriment of the franchisees. It recalls that Article 2 of the law of 5 August 1991 on the protection of economic competition states that

"All agreements between undertakings, all decisions by associations of undertakings and all concerted practices which have as their object or effect the prevention, restriction or distortion of competition in a substantial manner on the Belgian market concerned or in a substantial part of it are prohibited and in particular those which consist in: a) directly or indirectly fixing purchase or selling prices or other transaction conditions; d) applying, to trading partners, unequal conditions to equivalent services, thereby placing them at a competitive disadvantage; e) making the conclusion of contracts subject to acceptance, by the partners, of supplementary services which, by their nature or according to commercial usage, have no connection with the subject of these contracts."

S.A. Interdamo maintains that the adhesion agreements and sign contracts signed by S.A. ITM Belgium with numerous affiliates contain numerous restrictions of competition which have no other purpose than to reduce the costs of S.A. ITM Belgium without an equivalent consideration being granted to the franchisees who moreover only know balance sheets and negative results. These restrictive practices denounced by S.A. ITM can be summarized as follows:

1. Restriction of the freedom of action of members in advertising matters. Article 1.1., paragraph 2, of the sign contract stipulates that "The operating company undertakes to operate the business directly, without intermediary, tenant, or sub-concessionaire, at its full risk and for its entire profit but respecting all its contractual obligations and the ITM system."

However, according to S.A. Interdamo, this provision is contradicted by Articles 4.4 and 5.6 of this contract and by the fact that, in practice, S.A. ITM refuses franchisees to do advertising that would better meet the expectations of the population that makes up their clientele and their catchment area. 2. Obligation to bear costs related to services performed on behalf of ITM or its subsidiaries. Article 5.8. of the sign contract provides in its paragraph 2 that the expenses incurred by the majority shareholder member of the franchised company, during meetings organized by S.A. ITM Belgium, must be borne by this franchised company. S.A. Interdamo concludes that the aim thus pursued is to reduce the operating costs of ITM to the detriment of the operating companies. 3. Systematic refusal of alternative suppliers. While Article 6 of the sign contract expressly provides that the operating company is free to obtain supplies from suppliers outside the network if it can provide proof that the quality-price ratio is more advantageous, the applicant for interim measures affirms that, to do this, it is necessary to obtain the approval of S.A. ITM and that this is systematically refused even when the franchisees demonstrate that the selling price of a network product is higher than that practiced by competitors. 4. Obligation of price fixing. The adhesion contract develops in its Chapter I, entitled "fundamental principles" a policy called low prices. Paragraph 9 of this chapter specifies that "If the practice of low prices implies that it applies to all products, it must also be applied every day in all places by all members of the Network. (...) The maximum prices determined by the Network must (...) be applied by all (...)." Chapter II indicates (that) "For this purpose, the supply or referencing companies of the Network publish indicative schedules of maximum selling prices calculated as fairly as possible. A single price higher than the indicated maximum price calls into question these basic principles." S.A Interdamo considers that the fixing of prices as provided for by these contracts is contrary to Article 2 of the law of 5 August 1991 on the protection of economic competition in the same way as the Court of Justice of the European Communities and the European Commission decided in the Hennessy-Henkell case (O.J. No. L383 of 31 December 1980). MONITEUR BELGE — 12.02.2003 — BELGISCH STAATSBLAD 7155

5. Joint and several guarantee obligation. The adhesion contract, states as second principle (paragraph 6), that "the Network is, above all, a gathering of business leaders, at the head of human-scale enterprises... The head of each enterprise must be the majority contributor of capital and must engage his personal responsibility with regard to his obligations towards the Network or third parties..."

The applicant for interim measures concludes that the heads of franchised companies are required to respect the contractual conditions contrary to the law of 5 August 1991 on the protection of economic competition under penalty of sanctions. S.A. Interdamo further maintains that the majority of members experience difficult situations and are debtors vis-à-vis S.A. ITM Belgium and its subsidiaries including principally S.A. SCA. This situation generates a threat of seizure of the personal property of the managers and conditions the behavior of the franchised companies. 6. Interdependence. According to S.A. Interdamo, interdependence, the third principle of the adhesion contract, allows S.A. ITM and its parent company to indicate to the franchisees that their precarious situation is insignificant compared to the future and the profit of the Network namely ITME. The members and their companies must indeed prioritize the interests of the Network over their own interest. By this obligation included in the adhesion contract, the companies ITME and ITM Belgium obviously forget, according to S.A. Interdamo, the foundations of economic freedom and free competition. According to the terms of S.A. Interdamo, examples of companies in difficulty abound in this group which would clearly demonstrate that franchisees are neglected to serve the financial interests of ITM. The franchisees thus bear according to the applicant for interim measures, the economic, social and other costs of the bad policy of the Network justified by the interest of ITM. 7. "Third-Time" system. The adhesion contract provides in its Article 6 a rule called "Third-Time", which the member undertakes to respect. It is in fact for the members to devote one third of their working time, (i.e. two days per week), to the service of the Network, free of charge. The applicant for interim measures considers that this obligation has no other purpose than to give free labor to S.A. ITM and to allow it to thus reduce its fixed operating costs to the detriment of the franchisees who must bear the costs of these services without the slightest consideration. 8. Mechanism sanctioning the transgression of contractual obligations. S.A. Interdamo also considers that the mechanism of sanctions provided for by the adhesion and sign contracts in case of breaches of contractual obligations, which leaves the application of these sanctions to the discretion of S.A. ITM, violates the rules of the law of 5 August 1991 on the protection of economic competition. 9. Mechanism relating to the transfer of shares of the franchised company In its amplified complaint filed on 20 February 2002, S.A. Interdamo also invokes a new grievance resulting specifically from Article 5 of the adhesion contract which governs the transfer of shares of the franchised company and which grants a right of pre-emption to S.A. ITM. S.A. Interdamo considers that this right given to ITM can only be justified if the franchisor exercises it in a loyal manner, without resorting to mechanisms that aim to reduce the benefits of the franchisee for the sole profit of the franchisor and to allow the latter to obtain a point of sale at low price. V. Object of the application for interim measures

S.A. Interdamo requests that the president of the Competition Council suspend the rules in dispute, namely the adhesion and sign contracts, and impose penalty payments to encourage the incriminated parties to respect the decision to be rendered. At the very least, the applicant for interim measures requests the suspension of the adhesion contract especially its Articles 4, 5 and 10 as well as the corresponding provisions of the sign contract under penalty payments. VI. Markets concerned

The economic sector concerned by the present application is that of retail trade in non-specialized stores, predominantly food (NACE code 52.11). The market concerned relates to that of retail distribution of food products and non-food household items for current consumption, aimed at satisfying the recurrent needs of households. In this regard, the Competition Council in its decision of 1 October 2001 No. 2001-C/C-53 "Delhaize De Leeuw - Supermarkt DeWolf" already identified as the market concerned, the retail trade market for current consumption goods by also referring to the jurisprudence of the European Commission on the matter which considers that there exists, in the distribution market, a distinct market which corresponds to the retail distribution of food products and non-food household items for current consumption, aimed at satisfying the recurrent needs of households. Regarding the geographic delimitation of the market concerned, the Competition Council considered in this same decision of 1 October 2001 No. 2001-C/C-53 "Delhaize De Leeuw - Supermarkt DeWolf", like the European Commission, that the relevant geographic market in the retail trade market for current consumption goods, could cover either the entire territory, or one or more determined regions or a limited space. Neither the complainant, nor the Competition Service, nor the Body of rapporteurs pronounced on the relevant geographic market in the context of this procedure. A document in the investigation file and filed by S.A. Interdamo allows to note that the franchisees of this network are mainly established in the Walloon region and to a lesser extent in the Flemish region. In the absence of a more in-depth examination on this point and given the fact on the one hand, that the applicant for interim measures seems informally to have renounced its application, and on the other hand, that the general economic interest does not appear to be threatened, there is no need to define the geographic market in question. VII. Arguments of the incriminated parties S.A. ITM notes that the adhesion contract and the sign contract together establish a franchise between on the one hand the franchisor ITM and on the other hand the franchisees members of its network. The conclusion of these contracts is effected intuitu personae, that is to say in full "consideration of the personality of the majority shareholder member and manager of the operating company who, indeed, must possess professional experience, a capacity for adaptation to the constraints of the life of franchisees of the ITM network, a certain number of indispensable moral qualities and a sufficient personal financial standing. 7156 MONITEUR BELGE — 12.02.2003 — BELGISCH STAATSBLAD

S.A. ITM further considers that the litigation opposing it to S.A. Interdamo originates in the fact that Mr Langlet, in contravention of this principle of intuitu personae, transferred in disregard of the contractual framework provided for this purpose, all his shares in the company Interdamo to his wife and engaged with a major distribution chain competing with ITM, "Carrefour". When ITM wanted to exercise its right of pre-emption provided for in the adhesion contract, it saw this right contested by the Langlet spouses. As for the legal regime of the franchise, based on the teachings of the Court of Justice, ITM affirms that all the classic clauses of the franchise contract, in that they are indispensable to protect the know-how of the franchisor on the one hand and to organize the control of the identity and reputation of the network on the other hand, are not considered as restrictive of competition within the meaning of Article 81, § 1, of the EC Treaty. S.A. ITM also specifies that it holds a market share of [0-5%] on Belgian territory. Consequently, the contracts entered into with its franchisees are very largely below the threshold of 30% of market shares referred to in Community law by Regulation 2790/1999 on vertical restrictions. Therefore, according to S.A. ITM, they should benefit from the block exemption, unless it is established that they contain provisions set out in Article 4 of the said regulation. In addition, the contracts in question fall well below the 15% threshold defined by the Commission in its de minimis communication. As for the disputed clauses, S.A. ITM makes various observations:

- as for advertising: the provisions of the sign contract do not prevent the franchisee from programming its own promotional campaigns. The prior agreement of ITM stems from the right of the franchisor to control the commercial policy of franchisees in order to ensure that individually conducted promotional campaigns do not harm the image and reputation of the network. Regulation 4087/88 on the franchise contract expressly authorized such practices in its Article 3.1, g). For the rest, given that the contracts are exempt from territorial exclusivity, nothing prevents a franchisee from conducting its own advertising campaign and canvassing customers wherever it wishes. Competition between franchisees therefore plays freely. - bearing of costs: by signing the contract, the member marks his adherence to this principle which is again inherent in the very idea of a franchise contract (which groups several independents under a common sign). The restriction, purely contractual, cannot be qualified as a restriction of competition. - alternative suppliers: to develop a supply network, ITM invested heavily to establish a network of "affiliated companies" that offer specific services to the ITM network and achieve the conditions of quality and low prices. For reasons of profitability and balance of the organization, ITM indicates to franchisees that the said companies must be chosen preferentially for their supply. In this regard, the Pronuptia and Gottrup-Klim judgments recognize that such a clause must be admitted when it is necessary to protect the reputation of the network or when the objective pursued is not to jeopardize both the proper functioning of the cooperative and its contractual power vis-à-vis producers. Moreover, ITM does not prohibit its franchisees from having other suppliers but asks them to prove that the product corresponds to the price and quality conditions specific to the network. In practice, adds ITM, all points of sale have their small "direct" suppliers for the supply of certain "proximity" products without this being the subject of censure. - fixing of maximum prices: the low price policy, which implies not exceeding the maximum prices set by the network, is also a characteristic of the ITM network. S.A. ITM specifies that the determination of maximum prices in vertical relations between undertakings does not constitute a restriction of competition prohibited by Article 81, § 1, EC. Competition plays for the fixing of lower and not higher prices. Consequently, maximum prices are considered as pro-competitive. The franchisee is free to set its prices at the desired level, while respecting the maximum price, and therefore to compete with other franchisees within the network. The franchisees agree not to make an excessive profit to the detriment of consumers. S.A. ITM further specified that in practice, maximum prices are rather recommended prices which are not uniformly respected, without any sanction being taken. - joint and several guarantee: the philosophy of the franchise contract is to promote private initiative while making franchisees benefit from the advantages of a sign and know-how. As such, they commit themselves personally vis-à-vis the network and third parties. This is a contractual provision to which the franchisee freely subscribed upon conclusion of the contract. - interdependence: S.A. ITM recalls that a franchise system is by definition based on the idea of interdependence between franchisees who share the same sign in order to maintain a certain harmony within the network that gives it its identity. ITM specifies that there is no restriction of competition in the principles of the adhesion contract that would go beyond what is indispensable to ensure the proper functioning of the franchise. - discretionary sanctions: S.A. ITM affirms that the sanctions referred to in the adhesion and sign contracts are in accordance with the right reserved to the franchisor to "take the appropriate measures to preserve the identity and reputation of the network which is symbolized by the sign." In this sense, it is constant that admissible are the obligations imposed on resellers to cooperate in the development of the network, and to conform to the commercial methods developed by the franchisor. This is not an infringement of Article 2 of the law, but indeed a strictly contractual obligation which binds the signatory of the contract and which entails the termination of the latter when it is not respected

S.A. ITM also points out that the subject of the present procedure is if not identical, at least similar to the judicial procedures that were conducted between the parties. VIII. In law

For interim measures within the meaning of Article 35 of the law on the protection of economic competition to be pronounced by the president of the Competition Council, three conditions must be cumulatively met:

1. the existence of a complaint being the subject of an investigation; 2. the prima facie existence of an infringement of the law; 3. the probability of serious, imminent and irreparable prejudice in the case of the applicant or of a nature to harm the general economic interest. 1. Existence of a complaint being the subject of an investigation

A complaint was filed by S.A. Interdamo against S.A. ITM Belgium and SCA Produits régionaux on 18 January 2002 and was registered at the secretariat of the Competition Council under reference CONC-P/K-02/0007. MONITEUR BELGE — 12.02.2003 — BELGISCH STAATSBLAD 7157

The complainant is an undertaking within the meaning of Article 1, a), of the law, which justified a direct and current interest at the time of filing the complaint and the application for interim measures. Indeed, as a member of the Network of franchisees set up by S.A. ITM Belgium and its subsidiaries, it considered having been prejudiced by the establishment of a cartel prohibited by Article 2 of the law by means of these adhesion and sign contracts. These would contain provisions which would have the object of limiting the commercial policy, not only of the interested party, but also of the other members. S.A. Interdamo further affirms that it has experienced significant difficulties since 28 November 2001 following the prohibition made to its managing director to attend a monthly meeting of franchisees at ITM headquarters. These difficulties result, according to the applicant for interim measures, from the absence of transmission by ITM of important and necessary information for the operation of its point of sale (such as the prices of a series of goods) and delivery times not respected. S.A. ITM contests the admissibility of the present application on the grounds that parallel procedures have been introduced before the courts of the Judicial Order and the national competition authorities concerning a dispute and questions which seem to be if not identical, at least similar. The existence of such concomitant procedures does not obstruct the competence of the Competition Council and its President insofar as neither the thing requested, nor the law invoked, nor the parties present are the same: the actions move on different planes which do not overlap (Explanatory Statement, Parl.Doc., Ch.Repr., Ord.sess. 1989-1990, No. 1282/1, pp.33, 34). Various decisions on interim measures pronounced in accordance with Article 35 of the law of 5 August 1991 on the protection of economic competition explain why the risk of contradiction does not exist since the grounds of the decisions pronounced by the Courts and Tribunals on the one hand, and by the Council on the other hand are necessarily different. The law on the protection of economic competition alone is capable of founding a decision of the Competition Council and its president.The president of the Competition Council may thus, pursuant to article 35 of the Act, take provisional measures intended to suspend restrictive competition practices that are the subject of an investigation, if it is urgent to avoid a situation likely to cause serious, imminent and irreparable harm to the undertakings whose interests are affected by these practices or to harm the general economic interest, notwithstanding the existence of any judicial proceedings or decisions.

If, in the context of parallel proceedings, the operative part of the application filed by Interdamo is similar, the intervention of the Competition Council—and of its president in the context of provisional measures—takes place within the framework of objective proceedings concerning legality and the protection of economic competition and not within the framework of subjective proceedings settling particular disputes or establishing the right of a party.

The interests protected are therefore above all public and not private, which moreover justifies the erga omnes nature of the decisions of the Council, which must be published in the Belgian Official Gazette pursuant to article 41 of the Act, unlike decisions rendered by the Courts and Tribunals of the Judicial Order.

Furthermore, the (president of the) Council, when called upon to rule on the particular matter falling within its jurisdiction, has in particular the reasoned report of the Corps of Rapporteurs drawn up at the end of an in-depth investigation into the possible existence of a violation of the rules of economic competition, which the courts of the Judicial Order called upon to rule on possible violations of subjective rights do not have.

As they are courts belonging to distinct orders, the material jurisdictions do not overlap and the decisions have a fundamentally different scope.

The application for provisional measures filed by S.A. Interdamo is therefore admissible.

2. Prima facie existence of an infringement of the Act (article 2)

Given the specificity of the procedure provided for in article 35 LPCE, it is necessary to verify whether, on the basis of the elements of the investigation file available, there exists prima facie an infringement of the Act of 5 August 1991 on the protection of economic competition. The alleged infringement must thus have a plausible character: however, it is not necessary at this stage of the procedure to establish the existence of an infringement of the competition rules with the same degree of certainty as that required for a decision on the merits.

Examination of the investigation file and the documents in the proceedings reveals that S.A. Interdamo merely invokes grievances and advances assertions without providing evidence establishing the existence of a prima facie infringement of the Act of 5 August 1991 on the protection of economic competition.

This applicant for provisional measures did not, moreover, consider it necessary to appear at the hearings of the Competition Council to substantiate its accusations and even sent a fax on the day of the hearing scheduled to enable it to develop its grievances stating that it did not intend to pursue its application for provisional measures.

Moreover, it must be noted that S.A. ITM produced documents (including advertisements from members, including those emanating from the applicant for provisional measures), demonstrating on the basis of current elements of the file that these grievances are not relevant.

As nothing permits, at this stage, to conclude that there exists a prima facie infringement, it is appropriate to note the lack of foundation of the application for provisional measures filed by S.A. Interdamo.

Given that one of the conditions for granting provisional measures is lacking, there is no interest in examining those relating to serious and irreparable harm, and to urgency or that aimed at verifying whether the general economic interest is not harmed.

For these reasons,

We, Patrick DEWOLF, vice-president of the Competition Council acting as president,

Declare the application for provisional measures filed by S.A. Interdamo and registered under reference CONC-V/M-02/0008 admissible but unfounded;

Thus decided on 13 June 2002.

(3164)

Municipality of Beernem

Special development plans—Public inquiry

The college of mayor and aldermen of the municipality of Beernem brings to the general knowledge of interested parties, in accordance with the provisions of the decree concerning spatial planning coordinated on 22 October 1996, that the draft S.D.P. "Oudevaartstraat" was provisionally adopted in the session of 16 December 2002 of the municipal council.

This S.D.P. is available for inspection by anyone for thirty days, at the municipal hall of Beernem, Bloemendalestraat 112, technical services office, all working days from 9 to 12 o'clock, from Monday 17 February 2003.

Any objections or comments must be submitted in writing to the college of mayor and aldermen and received before the closing date of the public inquiry, Wednesday 19 March 2003.

Beernem, 4 February 2003.

(19089)

MONITEUR BELGE — 12.02.2003 — BELGISCH STAATSBLAD 7163

"Bloemendale" was provisionally adopted in the session of 16 December 2002 of the municipal council.

Beernem, 4 February 2003.

(3165)

Municipality of Berchem-Sainte-Agathe

Special land use plan—Notice of inquiry

The college of mayor and aldermen informs the population that the basic file of the special land use plan "Porte de Ville - Ilot 53" for the zone delimited by the axis of the railway line, the axis of the Chaussée de Gand, the axis of the Avenue Charles-Quint and the axis of the Chaussée de Zellik.

Established in accordance with legal provisions and provisionally adopted by the municipal council, in session of 30 January 2003, is submitted to inquiry from Monday 3 February 2003, to Wednesday 5 March 2003 inclusive.

Said plan is deposited for the attention of the public at the municipal hall, Avenue du Roi Albert 33, urban planning service, office 17, from Monday to Friday from 8:30 a.m. to 12 noon, as well as on Thursdays between 5 and 8 p.m. Technical information only on Thursday.

Public information session on Thursday 20 February 2003, at 7:30 p.m., in the multipurpose hall located on the 2nd floor, Rue des Alcyons 23, (accessible to everyone).

Anyone who has complaints or observations to make regarding these plans may submit them in writing to the college of mayor and aldermen or orally to the official designated by the public authority charged with the inquiry at the latest on 5 March 2003.

Sint-Agatha-Berchem, 31 January 2003.

(3166)

Municipality of Sint-Agatha-Berchem

Special development plan—Notice of inquiry

The college of mayor and aldermen brings to the knowledge of the population that the draft special development plan "Stadspoort-Wooneiland 53" for the area delimited by the axis of the railway, the axis of the Gentsesteenweg, the axis of the Keizer Karellaan and the axis of the Zelliksesteenweg.

Established in accordance with the provisions of the law and provisionally approved by the municipal council in session of 30 January 2003, between Monday 3 February 2003, and Wednesday 5 March 2003, inclusive, is subject to an inquiry.

Said plan is deposited for inspection by the public at the municipal hall, Koning Albertlaan 33, 1082 Sint-Agatha-Berchem, urban planning service, office 17, from Monday to Friday between 8:30 a.m. and 12 noon, and every Thursday between 5 and 8 p.m.

Technical information only on Thursday.

Public information session on Thursday 20 February 2003, at 7:30 p.m., in the multipurpose hall located on the 2nd floor, Alcyonsestraat 23, (accessible to everyone).

Anyone who has complaints or observations to make regarding these plans may submit them in writing to the college of mayor and aldermen or orally to the official designated by the public authority charged with the inquiry, at the latest on 5 March 2003.

Sint-Agatha-Berchem, 31 January 2003.

The agenda is as follows:

1. Draft merger by absorption of the Société anonyme "European Parking Control" by the société anonyme "Société immobilière Palazzo", established in accordance with article 693 of the Companies Code, deposited at the registry of the Brussels Court on 9 December 2002. Possibility for shareholders to obtain a copy of said document. 7164 MONITEUR BELGE — 12.02.2003 — BELGISCH STAATSBLAD

2. Decision on the merger within the meaning of articles 676, 682 and 719 to 727 of the Companies Code, by absorption by the société anonyme "Société Immobilière Palazzo" of the société anonyme "European Parking Control", by absorption of the latter of which it holds all the shares and which transfers to it the entirety of its assets on the date of this merger, and this, on the basis of a balance sheet as at 30 September 2002. From an accounting and tax perspective, the merger takes effect on 31 December 2002 at midnight.

an accounting statement of each of the companies as at 30 September 2002.

Hotel Chambord, société anonyme, rue de Namur 82, 1000 Bruxelles

Ladies and Gentlemen shareholders are requested to attend the extraordinary general meeting of the company on 28 February 2003, at 5 p.m. at the office of notaries Raucq and Maquet, at 1000 Bruxelles, Rue du Monastère 22.

Agenda:

1.1. Report established in accordance with article 559 of the companies code. 1.2. Modification of article 3 of the articles of association to replace it with: "The company's object is both in Belgium and abroad for its own account or for the account of third parties or in partnership with third parties: - all participations or takings of industrial, commercial, financial, movable or immovable property participations, relating directly or indirectly to the management and operation of all establishments or enterprises; - the creation, development, renovation, interior design, decoration, transformation, finishing, construction, purchase, sale, franchising, and all real estate operations relating to any type of building; - import and export, trade in all its forms of wines and other items relating to the horeca sector, leisure, sports activities such as horse riding and horse racing. - all marketing service provisions." - The company may provide for the administration, control of all related companies or with which there is a participation link and all others, and grant all loans or guarantees to them, in whatever form and for whatever duration. It may participate through investment in all businesses, enterprises, associations or companies with an identical, analogous or similar object, or which are of a nature to promote the development of its enterprise. In general, the company may realise its corporate object directly or indirectly and in particular conclude all agreements of association, rationalisation, collaboration, any employment or enterprise contract, lend its financial support in any form whatsoever, carry out all work and studies for any enterprise, association or company in which it has taken an interest or to which it has provided its financial support, sell, purchase, guarantee, lease or rent any tangible or intangible property, movable or immovable, lease or farm out all or part of its business. 2. Modification of the company name to "Bel gestion". 3. Transfer of the registered office to Uccle (1180 Bruxelles), Avenue d'Andrimont. 3. 4. Adaptation of the articles of association to the companies code: consequently, proposal to adapt the following articles of the articles of association to replace references to the coordinated laws on commercial companies with references to the companies code: articles 7, 9, 20, 29 and 31. 5. Other modifications to the articles of association to modernise and/or simplify the wording as well as to renumber the articles and restructure their presentation articles 2, 4, 5, 6, 11, 12, 13, 14, 15, 17, 18, 191, 20, 21, 26, 27, 28 and 30. Creation of an article 32 to organise judicial jurisdiction and an article 33 to organise election of domicile. 6. Powers to be conferred on the board of directors for the execution of resolutions to be taken on the foregoing objects and to the representative(s) to be designated for the purpose of carrying out all subsequent formalities.

To be able to attend the meeting, bearer shareholders are requested to deposit their bearer securities at the registered office, at the latest five clear days before the meeting; in accordance with article 21 of the articles of association, any shareholder may be represented at the general meeting by a special proxy holder, provided that the latter is himself a shareholder. Any shareholder may, upon simple request, obtain free of charge at the registered office a complete copy of the documents referred to in point 1 of the agenda.
